extra level is known in exactly one place, `data/csv_loader.py::csv_root`, which appends
`metadata/` when it exists and falls back to `csv_dir` when it does not.

That fallback is load-bearing. `CsvLoader.find_csv` returns `None` for a missing directory
rather than raising, so a path wrong here would have produced a **silently empty store**
instead of an error -- so the move was checked by counting what loaded (18 heroes, 145
comps, 133 loadouts, 1105 enemy comps, 8 corpses) and not only by the suite going green.

**Only three of the eight are read by code**: `HEROES/`, `COMPS/`, `ENEMY_COMPS/`. The
other five are reference material -- `data/store_cache.py`'s digest stats them so an edit
still invalidates the cache, but no loader opens them. Worth knowing before anyone spends
time maintaining them.

Verified: 1214 tests, `train.py --check` passes, and the bridge dry run over
`raidsnaps2` is unchanged at 74/62/12.

## The hidden +5 ACC is a roll floor, not an accuracy bonus (2026-08-19)

`engine/resolution.py` carries `HIDDEN_ACC_BONUS = 0.05`, added to accuracy before a clamp,
sourced from the Houndmaster hero doc: *"a hidden +5 ACC modifier always applies to any
hero/enemy attack so an attack always has a minimum 5% chance to hit, regardless of the
target's DODGE"*. **The observable effect is real. The implementation is a different
mechanic, and the difference is measurable.**

The game's to-hit function decides in this order: `roll <= cutoffAlwaysMiss (0.0)` misses;
`roll >= cutoffAlwaysHit (0.95)` **hits whatever the chance is**; otherwise it hits if
`roll < chance` -- and **the chance is never clamped**. There is no `+0.05` in the
function. The 5% floor comes from the top 5% of the roll range always hitting.

    game:  P(hit) = 0.05 + min(0.95, raw)        raw = ACC - DODGE
    sim :  P(hit) =        min(0.95, raw + 0.05)

Identical for `raw <= 0.90`. Above it they diverge, and the sim under-hits -- precisely on
the high-accuracy attacks a policy prefers.

### Three independent kinds of evidence

**The code** (`FUN_1405aa7f0`). **The chances**: the 166 real `(chance, roll)` pairs in
`archive/traces/*.log` reach **1.05**, which the sim's 0.95 clamp forbids outright. **And a
natural experiment**, using the fact that a hit is followed by a `Battle Random int range`
damage roll while a miss is followed by the turn ending:

| group | n | outcome |
|---|---|---|
| `roll >= 0.95` and `roll > chance` | 6 | **6/6 hit** -- damage rolled every time |
| `roll > chance`, `roll < 0.95` | 17 | **17/17 missed** -- no damage roll |

Six attacks that the chance alone says should have missed, and every one hit.

### The cost, measured on those samples

24 of 166 (14.5%) differ; largest gap **5.0pp**; mean P(hit) **0.8580 game vs 0.8512 sim**.

**Applied, and measured.** `compute_hit_chance` now returns a bare, unclamped
`ACC - DODGE`; `resolve_to_hit` holds the three-way decision and both call sites use it
(`resolve_attack`, and the riposte in `combat_engine` -- the latter by consistency rather
than separate validation, since the corpus does not distinguish riposte rolls).

`tools/eval_tutorial.py --episodes 50`, identical seeds and scenario either side of the
change, so the only variable is the rule:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `best_model` SUCCESS | 62.0% | **70.0%** |
| `best_model` completed | 66.0% | 72.0% |
| `best_model` deaths / run | 0.04 | 0.02 |
| `best_model` retreats / run | 0.34 | 0.28 |
| `best_model` final stress | 18.7 | 17.7 |
| tutorial specialist SUCCESS | 98.0% | **100.0%** |
| tutorial specialist deaths / run | 0.02 | **0.00** |
| tutorial specialist steps | 10.2 | 9.7 |

**This is not the policies getting better.** Neither checkpoint changed; the physics did.
Attacks that used to miss now land, so fights end sooner and the party takes less damage
on the way. The specialist's last death in 50 episodes is gone and it now completes the
Old Road perfectly.

**It is also an argument for a retrain, not a proof of one.** Both checkpoints were
trained against the under-hitting rule, so they are now very slightly off-distribution --
in the direction of being too cautious. The effect is small (mean P(hit) moves 0.8512 to
0.8580) and the honest position is that nobody knows whether it is worth 3M steps until
somebody measures a retrained checkpoint against these two on this harness.

### And the base term is the torch bracket (F5)

Resolving what the to-hit function *does* add turned up `FUN_1406053c0`: the **torchlight
range-table lookup**. Its two comparison branches are `lowest_excluded` itself -- `lower <=
t < upper` when clear, `lower < t <= upper` when set -- confirming how the project already
reads that flag. The returned struct's stride is `0x30` = the 12 floats of each bracket's
`value` block in `rules.json`, in file order, so offset 8 is **`monster_attack_increase`**.
That is the base added to attacker accuracy, gated on the attacker's type. Whether
`engine/` applies it at all is open. Do not confuse its 5.0 in the 25-50 torch bracket with
the 5 in the paragraph above; they are unrelated.

## A fixture that handed back a half-built env (2026-08-19)

`tests/env/test_training_torch_and_doom.py` went intermittent: about two failures in five
full-suite runs, passing in isolation every time, and passing serially every time.

**The fixture is module-scoped and did not reset.** `ExpeditionSequenceEnv.__init__`
builds no engine -- `reset()` does -- and three tests in that file use the fixture without
resetting it. One of them, `test_the_enemy_slot_grew_by_exactly_one`, calls `_get_obs()`,
which goes straight to `self.engine.get_current_actor_id()`. Serially they were rescued by
whichever earlier test happened to reset the shared fixture first. That is an accident of
ordering, not a guarantee.

`pytest.ini` runs `-n auto`, and xdist's default `--dist load` hands individual tests to
whichever worker is free -- so the test can land on a worker where nothing has reset the
env, and `self.engine` is `None`. Adding an unrelated test file (`tests/tools/test_pe_anchors.py`)
changed the scheduling enough to start surfacing it.

The fix is in the fixture, not in the three tests: a fixture that returns an object whose
`.engine` is `None` is the defect. Both torch tests that skip the reset are unaffected --
one asserts the shape of 2000 draws, the other reseeds `_sampler_rng` itself.

**How to reproduce this class of bug in seconds, not runs.** The whole-suite symptom is
useless for debugging; the file alone under xdist is not:

```bash
python -m pytest tests/env/test_training_torch_and_doom.py -n auto   # failed 3/3 in 2.2s
python -m pytest tests/env/test_training_torch_and_doom.py -n0       # passed, always
```

Any test that passes in isolation, passes serially, and fails intermittently under `-n auto`
is order-dependent through a shared fixture. Verified after: 5/5 file runs, 3/3 full-suite
runs, plus a full serial run, all green at 1212.

One loose end kept rather than tidied away: the earlier full-suite failures named
`test_a_corpse_is_never_doomed`, which does reset itself, while the deterministic
file-level reproduction named `test_the_enemy_slot_grew_by_exactly_one`. Same file, same
class of dependency, and no failure of either in three full runs since -- but that is not
the same as having explained the first name, and if it returns the 2.2s reproduction above
is how to catch it.

## The debug log has no gate, and the accuracy ground truth was already on disk (2026-08-19)

Chasing what gates the combat telemetry ended somewhere better than expected.

`FUN_1405aa7f0` is the to-hit resolver and identifies itself: it logs `Chance to hit: %f`
and `Roll to hit: %f`, unguarded at the call site. The printer is `log_info` -- it forwards
to a sink with the tag `0x696e666f`, ASCII `"info"` -- and that sink formats into a buffer
and calls **`OutputDebugStringA` unconditionally, before it looks at the tag at all**. The
tag only picks a severity for a second sink, the one that writes `app.log`.

So every `log_info` reaches the debug channel with **no launch flag**, and the five
`-enabledebug*` options are irrelevant to it.

**Which means `archive/traces/*.log` has held the game's own accuracy numbers since
2026-08-08**, captured for a different purpose and never searched: **166 (chance, roll)
pairs**, 34 distinct chance values from 0.555 to 1.05 -- so the chance is not clamped to
1.0 before logging. The roll printed is the **first of the three `Battle Random uniform`
draws immediately before it**, each carrying its seed, which fixes the draw order and the
stream position for every attack in the corpus.

That is the in-game validation the plan demands for the accuracy formula, available
offline, for free, today.

**What it does not overturn.** `Actor_DoSkill` appears 0 times in all four captures, so
the hero-skill half of `CLAUDE.md`'s note stands and behavioural cloning is not reopened.
The half that was wrong is narrower and is now corrected there: the channel carries more
than monster AI and the battle state machine.

### A broken measurement, and how it announced itself

The first pass reported 0 occurrences in all four captures and concluded the to-hit
function was dead code. The counting pipeline ended in `| bc`; `bc` is not installed on
this machine; the `|| echo 0` fallback then printed a clean zero for every pattern. Two
further conclusions were built on it before a second, differently-written check disagreed.

The rule worth keeping: **a negative result from a pipeline that has never once printed a
positive is not evidence.** The contradiction was caught only because a second measurement
was written a different way -- which is the argument for computing anything important
twice, by different means, before believing it.

## Reading the binary: the anchors, before the decompiler (2026-08-19)

New phase, and `DECOMP.md` is its ledger. The reconnaissance that decided it was tractable:
`Darkest.exe` is 19.3 MB with a 12.97 MB `.text`, **no PDB**, and **no `.bind` section** --
no Steam DRM wrapper, so it loads as a plain PE. MSVC left **596 RTTI type descriptors**
with full C++ names in the image (`Battle::CombatActionProcessor`, `Actor`, `MonsterClass`,
`AI::MonsterBrainClassLibrary`).

**But RTTI does not reach the maths.** The named classes are UI, town, quests and events;
`Resolve` matches nothing and there is no `Damage` or `ToHit` class, because combat
resolution is non-virtual code and plain structs, which carry no RTTI.

What reaches it is the thing this project already owns: **the name of every config key the
game reads**, enumerated across `data/*_loader.py` because the loaders parse the same files.
Nearly every key appears in the image exactly **once**, at its parse site. String -> address
-> the code that references it -> the struct field -> the readers of that field are the
formula. `tools/pe_anchors.py` does that with no decompiler installed: pure Python plus one
vectorised pass for RIP-relative displacements.

`DECOMP.md` carries the resulting anchor table. The clustering is the useful part: the
retreat, crit and stress keys all sit inside `0x140e7ef30`-`0x140e7f138` and the torch pair
at `0x140e6eb30`-`0x140e6eb40`, so each cluster is one `rules.json` parse function.

### Three ways to be confidently wrong, each hit on the first run

- **A key is stored in the form its own file uses.** JSON keys are bare, `.darkest` block
  headers keep the colon (`combat_move_skill:`), `.darkest` fields keep the dot
  (`.per_battle_limit`). Searching only the bare form reported a present key as absent.
- **"Absent" is only evidence for a key the shipped data contains.** Three keys reported
  absent on the first run were plausible names I had invented; the tool was correctly
  reporting that an invented string is not in the binary. `ANCHOR_KEYS` now holds only
  names verified against the real files. (The real retreat increment is
  `combat_retreat_bonus_chance_per_attempt`.)
- **A literal with no xref is not unreferenced.** `.per_battle_limit`, `.per_turn_limit`
  and `.is_crit_valid` sit in one block of `.darkest` field names that the parser walks
  from a single base pointer.

### The first finding, and the correction that made it worth having

The first pass said: **`excessCritMult` does not occur anywhere in the 19.3 MB image**, so
the game never reads it. True, and the conclusion drawn from it was wrong.

The game reads the multiplier under **`exessCritMult`** -- one `s`, at `0x140e7f128`. The
parser compares key names with `memcmp`, so `shared/rules.json`'s correctly-spelled
`excessCritMult` never matches, and the global keeps the hardcoded default. Read out of
the defaults function and the parser:

| data-file key | binary key | global | default | shipped | reaches the game? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cutoffAlwaysMiss` | same | `0x142ac44d8` | 0.0 | 0.0 | yes |
| `cutoffAlwaysHit` | same | `0x142ac44dc` | 0.9 | 0.95 | yes |
| `excessCritMult` | **`exessCritMult`** | `0x142ac44e0` | 0.0 | 0.0 | **no** |
| `excessCritMax` | same | `0x142ac44e4` | 0.2 | 0.2 | yes |

**Nothing behaves differently** -- default and shipped value are both 0.0, so the engine's
inert excess-crit overflow stays correct. What the entry buys is the trap: a modded
`rules.json` setting that key moves the **simulator**, which reads it by its correct name,
and not the **game**, which is looking for the typo. Mod support is queued for the next
phase, so that mattered enough to chase down.

And a rule for the method, which is the durable part: **an ABSENT verdict from
`pe_anchors.py` is a reason to go looking for a typo, not a licence to conclude a mechanic
is missing.** Pinned by `tests/tools/test_pe_anchors.py`.

### The map that came with it

The four functions that consume those constants split cleanly: `FUN_1405aa7f0` (8.5 KB)
and `FUN_1406f5730` (46 KB) read **both cutoffs and neither crit-excess constant** -- the
to-hit path; `FUN_1405c0260` and `FUN_1405ee530` read **the cutoff plus both crit-excess
constants** -- the crit path. `FUN_1405aa7f0` is the smallest and is where the universal
hidden **+5 ACC** either appears or does not. See `DECOMP.md` F2.

## The Old Road is a scenario now, not just a test rig (2026-08-19)

The tutorial is the only expedition Darkest Dungeon does not generate:
`scripts/starting_save/` is hand-authored and byte-identical on every new campaign. That
is what made it the corridor rig for `bridge/`; it also makes it the one expedition a
policy can be trained on **exactly**.

`data/tutorial_loader.py` reads the party out of the shipped files rather than restating
it — `persist.roster.json` gives Reynauld (Crusader, rank 1) and Dismas (Highwayman,
rank 2) at resolve 0, gear rank 0, 33/23 HP, 10 stress each, with their four equipped
skills as internal ids; `persist.raid.json` gives torch 100 and two provisions;
`persist.map.json` gives two battle tiles. The encounters are the weald's own two
`named:` mash entries, `tutorial_1` (Brigand Cutthroat) and `tutorial_2` (Brigand
Bloodletter + Fusilier) — the second matching what live play met in `rooB` exactly.

Three things worth knowing about how it is wired:

- **`ExpeditionSequenceEnv(fixed_scenario=...)` replays one scenario on every reset.**
  SB3 calls `reset()` with no options, so a scenario passed per-episode would train on
  sampled dungeons while reporting itself as a tutorial run. The dict is exactly what
  `reset(options=)` already accepted.
- **A scripted dungeon is scripted in its torch too.** `enemy_comp_names` names the
  encounter per combat and pins the torch with them. The Old Road ships `torchlight: 100`
  and is two fights long; sampling `TORCH_LEVEL_DECILES` over it would train the tutorial
  in a dark it never sees. A name that does not resolve **raises** rather than falling
  back to sampling — a typo that silently became "some random weald fight" would be a
  scenario nobody could reproduce, reported as the policy having got worse.
- **`named` stays out of the random pool** (`ENEMY_CATEGORIES_FOR_TRAINING`), so a
  scripted encounter cannot leak into a sampled expedition, or the reverse.

### The comp is registered, and deliberately NOT in the sampled pool

`COMPS/Comps/The Old Road/The_Old_Road.json` makes the tutorial party a first-class comp
for everything that works by name. **No row was added to `COMPS/Comps <hash>.csv`**, and
that is the whole point: `_build_hero_pool` and `pick_representative_comps` both take
`rng.sample` over `store.comps`, so one extra row changes *which comps every archived
`--comp-seed` selects* — silently invalidating the `--comps 12 --comp-seed 1` baseline
that CLAUDE.md tells everyone to compare against, and `tools/ab_compare.py` with it. The
tutorial party is reached by pinning it, never by drawing it.

The comp JSON is written in display names ("Tracking Shot") and resolved back through
the game's localization; the save carries internal ids ("take_aim"). Two paths to one
party, so `tests/env/test_tutorial_scenario.py` asserts they agree — that is the only thing
stopping the comp from quietly lying about what the tutorial equips.

### Measuring it is exactly reproducible, which nothing else here is

`tools/eval_tutorial.py` plays N seeded episodes of the fixed scenario. Unlike
`eval_expedition.py --full-expedition`, which draws a different dungeon every call, the
only thing varying is the combat RNG — so two checkpoints face identical fights and a
delta is a difference in play. `--self-check` plays one checkpoint as both arms and every
delta must print 0.00.

### The specialist, and what the general model was actually doing wrong

`python train.py --tutorial --timesteps 500000 --n-envs 8 --seed 0` — 500k steps, eval
reward flat at 25.0 +/- 0.2 well before the end. Graded against the general checkpoint
over **50 paired episodes** (same seeds, same two fights, so the only difference is the
play):

| | `best_model.zip` (general) | `dd_combat_ppo_tutorial.zip` | delta |
|---|---|---|---|
| SUCCESS | 62.0% | **98.0%** | **+36.0pp** |
| completed | 66.0% | **100.0%** | +34.0pp |
| retreats / run | 0.34 | **0.00** | -0.34 |
| deaths / run | 0.04 | 0.02 | -0.02 |
| final stress | 18.7 | **10.2** | -8.5 |
| reward | 20.9 | 24.7 | +3.8 |
| steps | 15.6 | **10.2** | -5.4 |

**The general model was not losing the tutorial — it was fleeing it.** A third of runs
ended in a retreat, which forfeits SUCCESS by definition and costs 20 stress a hero. That
is the whole gap: deaths were already near zero for both. Read alongside the live logs,
where `RETREAT on self` kept appearing in the advice for a 2-hero party at half HP, it
says the general policy has no idea how strong a rank-0 Crusader and Highwayman actually
are against one Brigand Cutthroat — reasonably enough, since a 2-hero party at resolve 0
is nothing like the comps it trains on.

On 20 **hold-out** seeds (500-519, never in the grading set) it repeats: SUCCESS 95.0%,
completed 100.0%, retreats 0.00 — so the 98% is not the grading seeds being kind. The
scenario is identical by construction, so this only varies the combat RNG; that is
exactly the variance the number is claimed against.

The specialist ends the raid at the stress it started with (10.2 against a starting 10)
and in two thirds of the turns. `--resume` fine-tuning from the general checkpoint was
**not** run: at 100% completed there is no headroom left to buy, and the remaining 2% of
SUCCESS is a single death in 50 episodes.

Point the bridge at it for a tutorial run:
`python bridge/run.py --boot --act --model models/dd_combat_ppo_tutorial.zip`. It is a
specialist and nothing more — it has seen exactly one party and two encounters, so it
should never be used to judge anything else.

## The bridge played a dungeon (2026-08-19)

`python bridge/run.py --boot --act` took the Old Road from the front end to
**Quest Complete** with both heroes alive. Booted into a new campaign, walked the
corridor, left rooms through the map panel, fought both battles, took the loot, and
finished. `captures/quest_complete.png` is the frame.

That is phase B's stated goal reached on the corridor rig. Nine live runs got here, and
every one of them was diagnosed from its own log plus, latterly, a banked frame: the
double map decode, the backwards-corridor arrival, the Move swap, the party-order button,
the false stall, the cross-combat `turn_key` collision, the ack window, the greyed-out
per-battle skill, and the OpenMP spin that made the game stutter.

**Two things are still open, and neither is a mystery any more:**

- **The curio press.** The party now walks up and stops in front of the tent — the walk
  is fixed, the press is not. No frame was banked on the completing run, so
  `_interact_curio` never reached its attempt loop: the room block either advised SKIP or
  the walk handed over to the route layer first. That is a different question from the
  five position theories, and the log will name it.
- **The end of a raid is an unmodelled screen.** `_send_miss.png` was banked at 21:36,
  the same minute as the Quest Complete frame: the bridge kept trying to play into a
  screen whose two buttons (return to the hamlet, and the map) it has never been shown.
  Nothing in `bridge/` plans for the raid ENDING, which is where the town phase starts.

## The input was not dropped: the button was greyed out (2026-08-19)

The 45 seconds of silence had a cause, and the operator named it before the frame did:
*"Tracking shot can be used once per battle only, it's trying to use it several times"*.

`highwayman/take_aim` — "Tracking Shot" on screen — is
`.per_battle_limit 1`. **The engine has enforced that since it was written**
(`_has_skill_uses_remaining`, checked in `get_valid_actions` and again at execution). But
`bridge/seed.py` rebuilds a `CombatEngine` from the save on *every turn*, and a fresh
engine starts every counter at zero. So the advisor kept recommending a button DD had
already greyed out, the click landed on nothing, and the save never moved. Three attempts,
45 seconds, no acknowledgement — identical in the log to a dropped keystroke, which is
what it was diagnosed as twice.

**13 hero skills carry a battle limit**, not one: `take_aim`, `barbaric_yawp`,
`blinding_gas`, `bolster`, `bulwark_of_faith`, `emboldening_vapours`, `heroic_end`,
`protect_me`, `revenge`, `solo`, `toxin_trickery`, `withstand`, `transform`. Only
Abomination's Transform also has `per_turn_limit`. The comment in
`_has_skill_uses_remaining` claimed Transform was the only one *at all* — a reader would
have taken the whole path for near-dead code. Corrected.

### The save does not record it, and that was checked rather than assumed

`skill_log` and `party_skill_log` are the only candidate fields, and both are `{}` in
**all 585 captured snapshots** and in a **live save read while a Highwayman had already
spent Take Aim that battle**. No hero record in `persist.roster.json` carries a use count
either. DD tracks per-battle limits in memory only.

So the bridge remembers instead, and remembers only what it can be sure of: **its own
sends, confirmed by the save**. `ActRunner.on_played` reports `(hero guid, skill id)` to
`Advisor.note_skill_used`, `build_engine_from_live(skill_uses=...)` writes the counts back
onto the seeded heroes, and the ledger clears per fight (keyed on `battles_completed`,
plus `forget_skill_uses()` whenever the party is standing in a room). A use that never
landed is never recorded — that would suppress a legal skill for the rest of a fight,
which is worse than the bug being fixed.

The gap this leaves is a human spending a limited skill by hand. The bridge then wastes
one turn's attempts on it and moves on, and it now says so with a screenshot.

## The bridge was spending two core-seconds per recommendation doing nothing (2026-08-19)

Reported as *"the game is also lagging much with the command"*. The first guess — that a
1 Hz poll decoding three DSON saves was too much — was wrong, and measuring said so: the
poll is mtime-gated (an idle tick is two `stat` calls), a decode is 17 ms, and one
`recommend()` is **1.5 ms**.

The cost is not the inference, it is what PyTorch does *after* it. The OpenMP pool
spin-waits ~200 ms on every thread at the end of each parallel region. Measured with
`time.process_time()` around one `recommend()` plus a 1 s sleep:

| threads | CPU burned | wall | |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10 (default here) | **1968.8 ms** | 1.01 s | 195% of one core |
| 1 | **0.0 ms** | 1.00 s | 0% |

Two core-seconds of spin per piece of advice, on the machine trying to render the game.
`quiet_torch()` sets one thread at both of the bridge's model loads; single-threaded
inference is also *faster* for a net this small (0.7 ms against 1.5 ms). Bridge-only and
deliberately so: `torch.set_num_threads` is process-wide and `train.py` wants every core.

## The curio: the third position theory, and the first one with a photograph (2026-08-19)

`captures/_curio_miss.png` — the frame banked automatically at the moment of a failed
press — shows the party standing several body-widths to the **left of the tent**, plainly
nowhere near it. Every theory before this was reasoned from the save; this one was looked
at.

So the arrival test goes back to walking **onto** the curio's tile (`ahead <= 0`), which
is the opposite of what it said two commits ago, and `CURIO_TRIES` goes 3 -> 6, because a
`WALK_HOLD` press covers well under a tile and three sub-tile nudges cannot close a gap
that wide. The extra reach is bounded by **position, not by count**: `_walked_past` stops
the search once the curio is more than a tile behind, so it can never walk on into the
door at the end of the corridor and leave the area it was searching.

## Some inputs really are dropped, and the log could not tell which (2026-08-19)

The run after the ack fix separated two failures that had looked like one:

```
[act] sent - confirmed by the save after 3.0s          <- landed, first try
...
[act] no save change in 15s - the input did not land (attempt 1/3)
[act] no save change in 15s - the input did not land (attempt 2/3)
[act] no save change in 15s - the input did not land (attempt 3/3)
```

Forty-five seconds with the save untouched is **not** a late acknowledgement — the same
plan, the same key, the same click coordinate had confirmed in 3.0s two turns earlier.
Something ate it.

Every candidate produces that identical log line: a tutorial popup holding modal focus,
the operator clicking their own terminal to read the log, the game disagreeing with
`current_actor` about whose turn it is (so the key selects a different hero's skill and
the click is not a legal target for it), or a backend this window happens not to accept.
The save distinguishes none of them. One frame distinguishes all four.

So the frame is banked at the **first** failed attempt — not the last, because by the
third the operator has reacted and whatever swallowed the input is off the screen. Same
move as the map panel and the curio: `captures/_send_miss.png`, one fixed name,
overwritten, gitignored.

**Focus is also re-checked per attempt now.** `_handle` cleared it up to 15s before the
retry goes out, and the most likely thing to happen in that window is exactly the thing
the operator is doing — reading the log in another window. A click sent then lands in
that window instead. Free when the game already has focus, and it waits rather than
firing blind.

Note what this does *not* claim. The 3.0s confirmation would have passed the old 6s gate
too, so this run does not prove the ack window was the problem; it proves the
distribution is wide (measured median 4.8s, max 11.5s) and that at least one genuine
drop exists on top of it. Two different faults, and now two different log lines.

## The ack window was shorter than the game's own write latency (2026-08-19)

Every send in the fourth live run reported the same pair of lines:

```
[act] no save change in 6s - the input did not land (attempt 1/3)
[act] sent (attempt 2) - confirmed by the save
```

Two for two, on turns that were then played correctly. A dropped keystroke is
stochastic; this was systematic, which is the signature of a threshold in the wrong
place rather than of an input problem.

**Measured against the captured corpus.** `archive/traces/raidsnaps*` are copies, so
their mtimes are the times the watcher saw each write, ~1s granular. Split by whether a
human decision sits inside the interval:

| gap between consecutive in-battle writes | n | median | p90 | max | over 6s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| machine only (a monster turn resolving) | 16 | 4.8s | 6.1s | 11.5s | 19% |
| human in the loop (a hero was next) | 175 | 5.6s | 11.5s | 97s | 45% |

And **DD does not write on every combatant's turn**: 175 of the 191 in-battle writes
land with a hero next to act. So the acknowledgement after a hero acts is that hero's
animation *plus every enemy turn before the next hero* — and `ACK_TIMEOUT = 6.0` was
sitting in the body of that distribution, not in its tail.

`TURN_ACK_TIMEOUT = 15.0` covers the whole observed range with headroom. It costs
nothing when the input lands (the wait returns on the change, not on the deadline) and
only delays the report of a genuine drop.

### The second action nobody could see

Worse than a false report: `before = self._save_sig()` was re-read at the top of **every
attempt**, after the settle. So the write that arrived late — the acknowledgement of the
first send — silently became the retry's baseline, and the retry then sent the same key
and the same click into a turn that had already been played. Nothing in the log could
say so, because from the retry's point of view the save had never moved.

A hero moving out and straight back again is exactly what a doubled `5` + rank click
does, and "the highwayman keeps looping on moving" was reported from live play in the
same run this was found. That is a hypothesis, not a closed case — the other candidate
is the policy itself, which was trained on an engine where a full party could never move
— but the double-press was real and is now impossible: **one baseline per call**, and a
save that moves between attempts cancels the retry instead of seeding it.

The ack latency is now printed on success (`confirmed by the save after 4.3s`), so one
live run replaces a corpus of human play with the bridge's own numbers.

## A move was marked against the enemy list (2026-08-19)

```
-> MOVE to rank 1
DIFFER played move highwayman (rank 2) - same kind, advice pointed at Brigand Bloodletter (slot 1)
```

The party did exactly what it was told. `advised_target` resolves a skill's footprint
through `engine._resolve_action_targets`, but `__move__` is not a skill id — the lookup
returns None, `ally` stays False, and the fallback matched the **destination rank**
against `engine.enemies`. So the advised set was a monster, the actor was the only guid
that visibly changed, the intersection was empty, and `judge` reported a disagreement on
every followed move.

A move is now judged hero-side and **against a rank**: the destination the advice names,
compared with the rank `observe` saw the actor end on. Both halves are pinned — a move
to the advised rank is a MATCH, a move somewhere else is still a DIFFER.

**It changes no archived number, and that was worth checking rather than assuming.**
`tools/agreement.py` imports `Session` from `bridge/run.py` and shares this marking, so
the first suspicion was that the **-2.9pp** the newly-legal Move action costs had been
measured through the bug. It has not: the bug can only fire when the advice *and* the
play are both moves, and over all three capture sets there is no such decision —
`best_model.zip`'s DIFFER breakdown has `attack -> move` 6, `support -> move` 2 and
`move -> attack` 2, and no `move -> move` row at all. The corpus replay tally is
byte-identical either side of the fix (69 resolved, MATCH 35 / DIFFER 30 / ? 4). The
defect is real and it fired live on the very first move the bridge ever played; it is
simply latent in what has been captured so far, which is exactly why acting live keeps
finding things a replay cannot.

## `.darkest` parser (`data/darkest_parser.py`)

Custom line-oriented key-value format: `combat_skill: .id "manacles" .level 4 .atk 115% .launch 32 .target 123`.
- `%` suffix → float/100
- `launch 4321` → digit-decoded to `[4,3,2,1]` (usable-from positions)
- `target ~123` → `~` prefix means AoE across those ranks; `@123` means the target list includes the performer's own rank (self-targetable heals/buffs) — both strip cleanly in either order (`~@123`/`@~123` both occur in game files)
- A key followed by multiple consecutive quoted values (`.effect "A" "B"`) parses to a `list[str]`; a single quoted value stays a plain `str` — don't assume `rec["effect"]` is always one type
- The regex tolerates zero whitespace between a closing quote and the next `.key` — at least one real game file (`base.effects.darkest:655`) has that typo, and requiring `\s+` there silently dropped the whole key
- Unquoted values can be bare word identifiers too (`.id cultist_brawler_A`, `.deathfx death_medium`), and booleans can be capitalized (`.accelerate_stall_penalty False`) — both parse correctly now. A negative lookahead (`(?!\.[a-zA-Z_])`) stops an *empty*-valued key immediately followed by another key (e.g. a self-only skill's blank `.target` right before `.is_crit_valid`) from swallowing that next key's name as if it were its own value
- **Two layouts, two entry points**: `parse_file` for the one-record-per-line layout above; `parse_blocks` for files that put the `type:` header on its own line with one `.key value` per following line (`scripts/map_generator.darkest`). `parse_blocks` rejoins the block and delegates to the same `_parse_line`. Using `parse_file` on a block-layout file fails **silently** — right record count, every field missing. See the dungeon-layout section above for the real bug this caused
- The unquoted-value trailing lookahead requires a **letter** after the next dot (`(?=\s*\.[a-zA-Z_]|$)`), not just any dot — an unquoted decimal number (`.atk 72.5%`) contains a literal `.` that's indistinguishable from "next key starts here" otherwise; the non-greedy value match would stop at `72` and silently drop `.5%`. Real bug found this way: 779 field instances across 240 real game files parsed as truncated bare ints instead of percent-decoded floats, mostly `.atk` (ACC values are frequently non-integer percentages) — see `ASSUMPTIONS.md`'s top section

## Engine architecture (`engine/`)

- `CombatantState` (`combatant.py`) — single struct for both heroes and enemies; `is_hero` flag plus separate `HeroDefinition`/`EnemyDefinition` references handle divergence (stress/affliction are hero-only, `actions_per_round` is enemy-only). No class inheritance split.
- `CombatEngine` (`combat_engine.py`, ~3200 lines, the core of the codebase):
  - `reset(hero_defs, enemy_defs, hero_positions=None, torch_level=100.0, hero_levels=None, hero_skill_ids=None, provisions=None, carry_over=None)` builds `CombatantState`s at positions 0-3 per side, computes initiative (`_compute_turn_order`), then immediately auto-resolves any enemy/stunned turns ahead of the first hero via `_advance_to_next_hero_turn()` — so a faster-than-the-party enemy can act before the first hero decision, matching the real game. `provisions` seeds `self.provision_inventory` (shared party inventory, consumed via free actions — see below); `carry_over` (list of `CarryOverHeroState | None`, index-aligned to `hero_defs`) seeds HP/stress/Death's-Door/mortality-tier from a previous combat within the same expedition — see "Full-expedition simulation" below. Both default to `None`/empty, behaviorally identical to before either param existed, same convention as `hero_levels`/`hero_skill_ids`.
  - `get_current_actor_id()` returns whose turn it currently is (or `None` if combat is done) — **always query this rather than picking a hero on your own**; the engine owns turn order via `current_turn_index` into `self.turn_order`, a single unified initiative queue mixing heroes and enemies (SPD+d8, descending), matching the real game's turn model.
  - `submit_action(action)` → `step()`: executes exactly one turn. If it's the current hero's turn, runs their submitted action, ticks their DoTs/death's-door, advances the index by one, then calls `_advance_to_next_hero_turn()` — which walks the queue automatically resolving enemy actions / stun-recovery / dead skips until it lands on the next living hero's turn (pausing there) or the round ends. `_end_of_round()` (buff/riposte/stealth duration ticks, mode stress, stall check, `self.round += 1`) runs exactly once per full initiative cycle, not once per hero action. `step()` returns a `StepResult(obs, reward, done, info)`.
  - `env/gym_env.py` calls `engine.get_current_actor_id()` directly — it has no independent hero-cycling logic of its own (a previous version tracked its own `_current_hero_idx` decoupled from the engine's real turn order; that was a real bug, fixed).
  - **Buffs are the universal modifier**: trinkets, quirks, and skill effects all resolve to buff IDs; `_apply_buff_to_stats` interprets `stat_type`/`stat_sub_type` (`combat_stat_multiply`, `combat_stat_add`, `resistance`, ...) against a `CombatantState`.
  - **A third inline-buff mechanism (`.buff_type`/`.buff_amount`) now applies.** Distinct from both `.buff_ids` (a named `store.buffs` reference) and the `combat_stat_buff 1`+specific-field pattern (`INLINE_STAT_FIELD_MAP`) — `.buff_type` names the real stat_type directly (`hp_heal_received_percent`: Arbalest's "Arb Stacking Heal N", the actual mechanism behind `battlefield_bandage`'s stacking self-heal; `crit_received_chance`: Shieldbreaker's "Expose Crit Debuff N", Arbalest/Musketeer's "Grapeshot Vulnerability N"). Was completely unparsed (29 real occurrences); `EffectDefinition.inline_buff_type`/`inline_buff_amount` now synthesize a `BuffDefinition` and apply it through the existing `_apply_buff_to_stats`, same pattern as the pre-existing inline-stat-field branch.
  - **Shieldbreaker's Aegis Block (Serpent's Sway) — a genuinely new mechanic, not just a wire-up.** Real `.health_damage_blocks 2` field on "SB Aegis" (previously unparsed anywhere) grants stacking charges (`CombatantState.damage_blocks`, capped at `CombatRules.health_damage_blocks_max` — real `rules.json` value 2) that negate the next non-zero direct-damage hit entirely (`engine/resolution.py`'s `resolve_attack`, right after the `max(1, round(applied))` floor — every landed hit qualifies) — no HP loss, no Death's Door check, matching her own doc's "prevents damage/Deathblow check." Deliberately scoped narrow (matches "doesn't protect against Stress/DoT/Debuff/KB/Pull/Stun" — those never route through `resolve_attack`); also wired into the Riposte counter-attack's direct-damage application for consistency.
  - **Monster-type damage bonuses (`.monsterType`, e.g. Bounty Hunter's "Man Killer N" — his own doc's "+DMG vs Human") now apply.** Same conditional-damage-bonus shape as the `key_status` mechanism (`_compute_mark_damage_mult`, `target=="performer"`), but gated on the attack target's *permanent* monster-type tag (`EnemyDefinition.enemy_types`, e.g. `cultist_brawler_A`→`["man"]`) rather than a transient status — previously unparsed entirely (`EffectDefinition.monster_type_condition`), so this and 20 similar Beast/Unholy/Eldritch Killer effects across other classes were completely inert regardless of the real target.
  - **Whole-battle self-buffs (`buff_duration_type: "combat_end"`) now actually last the whole battle.** 52 real effects (Crusader's `"Bulwark N"`, Grave Robber's Toxin Trickery — `"Shadow Blood N"`/`"GR Self Speed N"`, Highwayman's `"Tracking Buff N"`, Leper's `"Leper Strength/Vulnerability/Protect N"`, Man-at-Arms' `"Bolster N"`, Plague Doctor's `"PD Vapours Buff N"`) carry a normal-looking `.duration` (often `1`) alongside `.buff_duration_type combat_end`, which overrides it entirely — the buff is permanent for the rest of combat, not a real short countdown. `buff_duration_type` was previously unparsed; `EffectDefinition.buff_duration_type` (default `"rounds"`, unchanged behavior for everything else) now gates both places a buff gets tracked for later reversal (`_apply_effect_to_target`'s inline-stat-field branch and its `buff_ids` branch) to skip expiry tracking entirely when set to `"combat_end"`.
  - **Mark/Vulnerable status (`is_marked`) now actually gets applied.** Real Mark-application effects (`"Mark Target"`, Occultist's `"Vulnerability Curse N"`, `"BH/Arb/HM Mark Target"`, boss "X Mark" skills — real field `.tag 1`, `.target "target"`, genuine 2-4 round `.duration`) previously did nothing at all — the engine keyed mark-application off `eff.key_status == "tagged"`, but `key_status` is a **different** mechanism (`_compute_mark_damage_mult`'s conditional damage-bonus-vs-marked check, e.g. Grave Robber's "GR Dagger Dmg Marked N"), which always targets `"performer"` — so the old code was flagging the *attacker* as Marked instead of the enemy. `EffectDefinition.is_mark_effect` (parsed from `.tag`) plus `CombatantState.mark_rounds_remaining` (ticked via `_tick_mark_durations`, same shape as guard/stealth duration tracking) fix both the wrong-entity bug and the missing expiry. `_compute_mark_damage_mult` also now checks any real `.keyStatus` value ("tagged"/"poisoned"/"bleeding"/"stunned"), not just "tagged" — Grave Robber's Thrown Dagger bundles both a tagged- and a poisoned-gated bonus in the same effect list.
  - **Conditional trinket/quirk buffs** (`BuffDefinition.rule_type`/`rule_data_float`/`rule_data_string`, real data verified against `shared/buffs/base.buffs.json`): 52% of all real buff entries (1060/2019 — 345 `TRINKET_`, 100 `QUIRK_`, 467 per-tier `TB` scaling variants) gate whether the buff is *currently* active — front/back rank, melee/ranged-only, HP-below/above-X%, at Death's Door, guarded, first-round-only, stress-above, or a target-status check (e.g. "Thirsting Blade"'s crit bonus is real but only vs a currently-bleeding target, not unconditional as previously assumed). `apply_trinkets_and_quirks` used to bake every buff in permanently at equip time regardless of `rule_type`. Now: `rule_type == "always"` buffs still apply immediately; anything else is deferred into `CombatantState.conditional_buffs` and toggled on/off every action via `_sync_all_conditional_buffs`/`_is_conditional_rule_active` (called from both `step()` and `_execute_enemy_skill`, so a hero's rank/HP/guard-gated buffs stay correct whichever side is acting) against `active_conditional_buffs` (buff id → applied delta, for exact reversal via `_reverse_buff_from_stats`). `afflicted`/`virtued` rule types are declared out of scope (they only ever gate the ~36 affliction/virtue *personality-type* buffs — Fearful/Paranoid/etc. — a separate unimplemented subsystem, see below); so are monster-matchup and town/camping rule types. A connected gap fixed alongside this: `crit_received_chance` (Man-at-Arms' Bellow) had no stat-type handler at all — `check_crit` now takes a `target` param and includes `target.crit_received_bonus` in the roll.
  - Effect resolution (`_resolve_effect`) runs after damage/hit resolution — stun, bleed, blight, push/pull, mark, stat buffs — each checked against target resistance via RNG.
  - Death's door: hero HP ≤ 0 → death's door flag; next hit is a deathblow unless `death_blow_resist` saves it. Enemies just die at 0 HP.
  - **`_execute_action` has a third branch for pure ally-buff skills, found and fixed after a genuinely severe bug.** Previously only `is_heal` (skill has a bare `.heal` field) vs. everything-else-goes-through-`resolve_attack` existed — but plenty of real ally-targeting (`targets_allies`/`_is_ally_skill`) support skills heal or buff purely via a *named effect* instead of the skill's own `.heal` field (Jester's `battle_ballad`/`inspiring_tune` → "Embolden Team"; Flagellant's `reclaim`/`redeem`/`endure`/`suffer` → "Flag Super Heal"/"Flag Grit"; likely others), so they fell through to the attack path and dealt the performer's **real weapon damage** to their ally targets — including the performer itself for AoE/self-inclusive skills. Confirmed via full-expedition replay: `"Jester hits Jester for 102"` from `battle_ballad`, whose real `.dmg` is `0%` (which means *no damage reduction at all*, not "-100%" — the misreading that let this hide). Now: `is_ally_skill and not is_heal` applies effects/movement only, no attack roll, no Riposte, no Guard redirect (mirroring how `_resolve_self_action` already correctly handled self-only skills). See `ASSUMPTIONS.md`.
  - **`get_valid_actions` now filters by `valid_modes`** (new `_is_valid_mode` helper, shared with `_check_valid_mode`'s execution-time check) — previously only checked at execution time, so Abomination's beast-only skills (rage/rake/slam) were offered as legal actions while in human mode (and vice versa for `absolution`), silently rejected on selection and wasting the whole turn.
  - **The `is_heal` branch never applied a heal skill's own `.effect` list — the single most impactful bug found in this project's later audits.** `_resolve_heal` only ever applied the bare `.heal` amount; nothing called `_apply_skill_effects` for it. Real, concrete cost: **Crusader's `inspiring_cry`** — the primary stress healer in most comps that carry a Crusader — delivers its actual stress-heal (`"Crusader HealStress N"`, 8 stress at max level) and torch gain (`"Crusader Light N"`) as named effects; its bare `.heal` is a token 1-2 HP. Before this fix, using Inspiring Cry only ever gave that trivial HP tick and relieved **zero** stress — invisible in isolation (the data-only test `test_stress_heal_effect_resolves` already confirmed `stress_heal=8` was parsed correctly, but nothing tested that combat actually applied it), and a very plausible systemic contributor to comps pinning at 200 stress project-wide. Also silently affected Plague Doctor's `battlefield_medicine` (`"Cure"`/`"Cureself"` — the real Bleed/Blight-clear side effect explicitly documented as its main value, not the "tiny direct Healing"), Antiquarian's `fortifying_vapours` (`"Fortify Resists N"` buff), Arbalest's `battlefield_bandage` (`"Arb Stacking Heal N"` buff), and Occultist's `wyrd_reconstruction` (`"Wyrd Bleed N"`, the real risky-heal bleed chance) — confirmed as the *complete* list via a project-wide grep for any hero skill with both a bare `.heal` and a `.effect` field. Fixed: the `is_heal` branch now calls `_apply_skill_effects`/`_apply_movement` per target and `_apply_self_effects` once, mirroring the ally-skill branch immediately below it. See `ASSUMPTIONS.md`.
  - **`_resolve_effect` never applied a named effect's OWN heal at all, in three distinct ways — all fixed together.** (1) `EffectDefinition.heal_min`/`heal_max` (a flat named-effect heal, e.g. Flagellant's on-death party heal `"Flag Last Gift"`) was parsed but never consulted anywhere in `_resolve_effect`. (2) `.heal_percent` (percent-of-max-HP, e.g. Redeem's real heal — `"Flag Super Heal N"`/`"Flag HealSelf N"`, ~33-50% max HP) wasn't even parsed into `EffectDefinition` at all. (3) `.dotHpHeal` (a heal-over-time/regen effect, e.g. Reclaim's real heal — `"Flag Healdot N"`) likewise wasn't parsed. Net effect: **Redeem — "arguably Flagellant's strongest skill" — and Reclaim healed for zero HP**, applying only their self-debuff/self-Bleed recoil. Fixed: `EffectDefinition` gained `heal_percent`/`heal_dot_amount`/`heal_dot_rounds`; `_resolve_effect` now applies flat-or-percent heals via `_apply_heal` and inflicts heal-over-time via a new `CombatantState.heal_dots` list, ticked by `_tick_heal_dots` at the same call sites as `_tick_dots`/`_tick_stress_dots`. Confirmed narrow real-data scope (`grep` across the whole install): only Flagellant and Color of Madness DLC content use either field.
  - **`_get_effect_target`'s `"performer_group_other"` spec only ever returned ONE ally, not the whole group — a second, independently severe bug found while fixing the above.** Any effect meant to hit "every other party member" (Abomination's Transform — `"beast_stress_party"` +8 stress human→beast, `"human_stress_heal_party_N"` −2 stress beast→human; Flagellant's death's-door-enter party heal; Arbalest's Rallying Flare stress-heal/buff) silently applied to just the first living ally found, every other time, for the project's entire history. Fixed: `_resolve_effect` is now a thin dispatcher — group specs (`"performer_group_other"`/`"performer_group"`) loop over every living member of the resolved side and call the (renamed) `_apply_effect_to_target` once per member; single-target specs resolve via `_get_effect_target` exactly as before. `_get_effect_target` itself no longer has a (now-dead, and misleadingly single-valued) group branch.
  - **Two entirely unwired Flagellant-specific trigger systems, found via a `HEROES/Heroes/*.md` doc audit.** `deaths_door:`'s real `.enter_effects`/`.enter_effect_round_cooldown` fields (a reaction fired once on *entering* Death's Door, distinct from the passive `.buffs` applied while there) and the standalone `death_reaction:` record type (fired once on actually *dying*) were both parsed by nobody and consulted nowhere — both are Flagellant-only in the real game (confirmed via `grep` across every hero file). Fixed: `HeroDefinition` gained `death_door_enter_effects`/`death_door_enter_round_cooldown`/`death_reactions` (a new `DeathReaction(target_allies, target_enemies, effects)` type), parsed in `HeroLoader._build_hero`; `CombatEngine` gained `_maybe_trigger_death_door_enter` (called from `_handle_deaths_door`/`_trigger_heart_attack`'s "entering" branches, gated by a new `CombatantState.last_death_door_enter_round` cooldown tracker) and `_maybe_trigger_death_reactions` (called from both hero-death branches) — the latter applies each reaction's effects to every living member of whichever side its own `target_allies`/`target_enemies` flag names (the group semantics live on the `death_reaction` record itself, since its effects' own `.target` is the single-target `"target"` spec). `flagellant_low_hp_reaction` (the real "below 40% HP: +20% DMG/+7% CRIT" self-buff) is a related, confirmed-real, but **not yet implemented** mechanic — its trigger condition (an HP-ratio check) isn't gated by anything in the effect record itself, unlike the death's-door/death triggers above, and needs its own investigation into how/where that threshold check should live; flagged in `ASSUMPTIONS.md`, not chased further this pass.
- `SeededRNG` (`rng.py`) — every random draw in the engine goes through this wrapper (`random()`, `uniform()`, `randint()`, `choice()`, `roll_d8()`). Never call `random` directly in engine code — this is what makes combat traces reproducible from a seed.
- `resolution.py` — accuracy (`compute_hit_chance`) and damage/crit resolution (`resolve_attack`), clamped by `CombatRules.cutoff_always_miss`/`cutoff_always_hit`.
- `stress.py` — `apply_stress` / `resolve_check`: at ≥100 stress, roll vs `affliction_base_onset_chance` for affliction, or `affliction_critical_success_chance` for virtue.

## Torchlight, monster AI, and generic buff resolution (`engine/combat_engine.py`)

- **Torchlight**: `CombatRules.darkness_table` (5 bands from `rules.json`, loaded via `data/rules.py`) is picked once at `reset(torch_level=100.0)` (`_get_darkness_band`, lower-inclusive/upper-exclusive) and applied as permanent stat deltas (`_apply_torch_modifiers`) — fixed for the whole encounter, no mid-combat light changes in this simulator. Monster damage can't be baked into `enemy.dmg_min`/`dmg_max` at reset because `_execute_enemy_turn` re-derives those from the skill's `atk_dmg_min`/`atk_dmg_max` every turn — the multiplier is stored (`self._monster_dmg_mult`) and applied at that point of use instead. Exposed via `env.reset(options={"torch_level": N})`.
- **Monster AI**: `store.monster_brains` (from `data/monster_brain_loader.py`) drives `_select_enemy_skill`/`_select_enemy_target` — a weighted lottery (`SeededRNG.weighted_choice`) over the enemy's own skills and candidate targets (heroes, or the enemy's own living side for friendly-targeting skills — see below), replacing what used to be `ed.skills[0]` always plus a uniform random target. Essentially every real skill-selection and target-selection desire type from `base.monster_brains.json` is modeled now (or confirmed data-equivalent to one that is, e.g. `preferred_skill`≈`random_skill`, `rank_target`≈`random_target`) — only `fill_ally_captor_empty_skill`/`_target` (The Hag's capture mechanic, out of scope) and the unreachable `next_initiative_target` aren't. See `ASSUMPTIONS.md` for the full per-type verification notes. Monster skill effects (bleed/mark/debuffs) are applied now too (`_apply_skill_effects`), not just raw damage.
- **Monster friendly targeting**: `SkillLevelData.targets_allies` (the `@`-in-`.target` signal) is computed for monster skills too now, not just hero skills — a monster skill with `@` in `.target` (e.g. Collector Shaman's `head_heal`/`head_buff`) targets the performer's own living side via `_select_enemy_target(..., is_friendly=True)` instead of the hero party. A skill with a completely empty `.target` (not even `@`, e.g. Cyst's `beholder_healself`) is self-only, resolved directly against the performer. `_execute_enemy_skill` (extracted from `_execute_enemy_turn`'s per-action body, shared with `_resolve_bonus_initiative`) branches on these before falling through to the normal hero-attack path.
- **Monster heal skills**: `SkillLevelData.heal_min`/`heal_max` are now the skill's real (previously-unparsed) `.heal N M` value — `atk_dmg_min`/`atk_dmg_max` hold the `.dmg`-derived attack range instead of overloading `heal_min`/`heal_max` as before. A monster skill with `heal_min is not None` heals its target (self, or an ally via friendly targeting) through `_apply_heal` instead of `resolve_attack`.
- **`bonus_initiative_desires`**: despite the name, **not** an initiative-roll modifier — a scripted bonus-action/skill-override system for 14/165 boss-tier brains (Prophet, Swine Prince, Cannon, Drowned Captain, Ancestor forms, Sapper, Hateful Virago, Skeleton Bearer). `MonsterBrain.bonus_initiative_desires` fires at `reset()`/`_advance_to_next_hero_turn()` (round-start) and `_end_of_round()` (round-finish) via `_resolve_bonus_initiative()`, executing through the same `_execute_enemy_skill()` helper as normal turns. 4/6 real trigger types modeled (`guaranteed`, `ally_actor_class_count`, `hp_ratio_threshold`, `last_skill`); `death` (Ancestor phase transition) and `ally_last_damaged` (redundant, Swine Prince only) are declared out of scope — see `ASSUMPTIONS.md`.
- **Generic buff resolution**: `EffectDefinition.buff_ids` (any `.buff_ids "X" "Y"` field) resolves against `store.buffs` and applies via `_apply_buff_to_stats`; if `EffectDefinition.duration` is set, tracked via `ActiveBuff` for reversal. `_tick_buff_durations` reverses *any* expiring buff now via `_reverse_buff_from_stats` (the exact inverse of `_apply_buff_to_stats`) — it used to only ever reverse stun-resistance buffs, a hardcoded special case. Death's door debuffs (`_apply_deaths_door_buffs`) reverse the same way, triggered from `_apply_heal` when `is_deaths_door` clears (`_reverse_deaths_door_buffs`).
- **Bleed/Blight/Horror DoT fixes**: `_tick_dots` used to re-roll `bleed_resist`/`blight_resist` on *every tick*, on top of the resist check already gating infliction in `_resolve_effect` — resistance only gates infliction in the real game (wiki-verified), so this silently under-dealt DoT damage project-wide for the project's whole history. Fixed — ticks now apply unconditionally once inflicted. **Horror** (stress-damage-over-time, e.g. Ghoul's `howl`, Madman's `accusation`) was separately unimplemented due to a parser key mismatch (`data/effect_loader.py` checked `stress_damage_per_round`, the real key is `.dotStress`) — same bug class as the historic `dmg_min`/`dmg_max` mismatch. Fixed: `CombatantState.stress_dots` (new, same shape as `bleeds`/`blights`) is inflicted in `_resolve_effect` (hero-only, gated by the effect's own `.chance` — no dedicated Horror-resist stat exists) and ticked via new `_tick_stress_dots`, routed through the existing `_apply_stress` helper so affliction/heart-attack triggers fire correctly; laudanum's real `.clearDotStress` cure is no longer inert. Found via a systematic real-`.darkest`-file-vs-loader key diff, which also surfaced three further real gaps, all now fixed too — see `ASSUMPTIONS.md` for the full investigation of each:
- **`extra_targets_chance`/`extra_targets_count`** (`SkillLevelData`, ~30 boss/named monster skills — Crone, Prophet, Ancestor, Brigand Sapper): a real chance-based "also hit one more distinct target" mechanic (count is always 1 in real data), wired into `_execute_enemy_skill` right after primary-target selection — each extra-target slot independently rolls its chance, and `_select_enemy_target` picks a genuinely different hero from the remaining pool.
- **`can_be_riposted`** (`SkillLevelData`, default `True`): a handful of monster self-destruct/flee skills (Bloated Corpse's `explode`, Crow's `escape`) flag this `False` in real data — now gates the `_maybe_trigger_riposte` call in `_execute_enemy_skill`.
- **`ignore_protection`/`ignore_stealth`** (`SkillLevelData`, hero *and* monster skills): wiki-confirmed real semantics — Grave Robber's "Pick to the Face" is "Armor Piercing" (`ignore_protection` skips the `target.prot` damage reduction in `resolve_attack`, `engine/resolution.py`); Highwayman's "Tracking Shot" (`take_aim`) is "Bypass/Remove Stealth". The monster-skill side of `ignore_stealth` (Crow's `distract`, Cyst's `beholder_gaze`/`burn`, Prophet's several skills) was the real, concrete bug — `_execute_enemy_skill`'s stealthed-hero targeting exclusion applied unconditionally before this, with no bypass for skills meant to reveal a hidden hero.
- **Resolve Level**: `reset(..., hero_levels: list[int] = None)` — Resolve Level 1-6 per hero maps to `CombatantState.skill_tier` (0-4) via `tier_idx = clamp(level-1, 0, 4)` — level 2→Tier II (idx1), level 4→Tier IV (idx3), level 6→Tier V/max (idx4) — a **deliberate simulator convention** (level and gear tier are decoupled progression systems in the real game — no official 1:1 table exists; the real weapon-tier-unlock table only gives a non-linear *minimum* resolve level per tier — 0/1/2/3/5 for tiers 1-5 — not a canonical "hero of level N owns tier N" mapping). `skill_tier` now drives both weapon/armour tier selection at `reset()` and `get_skill_data(id, level=...)` calls in `get_valid_actions`/`_execute_action` — previously **every** skill lookup hardcoded `level=4`, so non-max-tier heroes were structurally impossible. Also applies +10% resist (stun/blight/bleed/move/debuff) per level above 1 (wiki-verified). **`hero_levels=None` (default) must stay behaviorally identical to before this existed** — max tier, zero resist bonus; do NOT default the per-hero level to 6 internally, that already caused a real regression (silently added +50% resist to every default combat) once — see `ASSUMPTIONS.md`.

## Gym env (`env/gym_env.py`)

`DarkestDungeonEnv(render_mode=None)` loads the full `DataStore` once at construction (slow-ish — real game file parsing). `reset(seed=, options={"hero_ids": [...], "enemy_ids": [...]})` builds a fresh `CombatEngine`; default roster is 2 Crusaders vs 2 Cultist Brawlers if `options` omitted.

- **Action space: `Discrete(37)` with FIXED semantics** (`ACTION_*` constants in `env/gym_env.py`): `[0..27]` = `skill_slot * 4 + target_rank`, `[28..31]` = move-to-rank, `[32..35]` = provision (`PROVISION_ACTION_ORDER` — an explicit tuple, because `engine.IN_COMBAT_PROVISION_IDS` is a **set** whose iteration order must never assign stable indices), `[36]` = pass. `skill_slot` indexes the hero's **full** 7-skill movepool via `CombatEngine.get_hero_full_skill_ids()`, NOT the restricted 4-skill comp loadout — so slot *k* names the same skill for a given class no matter which 4 a comp equips (all 18 real classes have exactly 7 skills). This replaced a `Discrete(64)` that indexed into whatever `get_valid_actions()` happened to return that turn, where the same index meant a different action every turn. `_rebuild_action_table` builds an `index -> Action` map each turn; `step()` looks up that map and falls back to the engine's own `__pass__` for an unmapped index (it no longer clamps — the old `min(action, len(valid_actions)-1)` silently rewrote an out-of-range choice into whatever was *last* in the list, the confirmed source of a "shuffles positions forever instead of attacking" pathology). **Action masking is still required, not optional**: `env.action_masks()` returns a `(37,)` bool array, True exactly on indices that map to a currently-legal action. `train.py`/`eval_expedition.py` use `sb3_contrib`'s `MaskablePPO` + `ActionMasker(env, mask_fn)` — **`env.gym_env.mask_fn`, never an inline lambda and never the string form.** The string branch does `getattr(self.env, name)` (an already-bound method) but then also calls it with `self.env` as an explicit arg, raising `TypeError: takes 1 positional argument but 2 were given`. The obvious callable, `lambda e: e.action_masks()`, has a second and worse failure — see "Two of three training modes were dead" below.
- **Observation space: `Box(shape=(324,))`** (331 for `ExpeditionSequenceEnv`, +7 expedition-context floats) — **rank-indexed**, so it lines up with what an action means: 4 hero rank slots × (21 scalars + 18-class one-hot) + 4 enemy rank slots × (14 scalars + 9-enemy-type one-hot) + 7 skill slots × 10 features + 6 globals (`GLOBAL_FEATURES`). Derived from the layout constants, never hardcode the width. Hero slots carry **`is_current_actor`** plus stun/mark/guarded/guarding/riposte/stealth/afflicted/virtuous/Death's-Door flags, DoT totals, Aegis blocks, and **live dodge/prot/speed/crit** — encoding live stats is what makes buff/debuff *effects* observable without enumerating buff ids, and is how the conditional trinket/quirk system actually reaches the policy. Enemy slots carry corpse/stun/mark/stealth, DoTs, size, live stats, `actions_per_round`, and the real `enemy_types` one-hot. Skill slots describe the acting hero's movepool (exists / legal-now / ally-targeting / heal / AoE / atk / dmg_mod / crit / stall-invalidating / free-action). The previous 50-float version encoded only `[hp%, position, stress, is_dd, is_dead]` per hero and `[hp%, position, is_dead]` per enemy — **no current-actor signal, no class or enemy identity, and no status of any kind**, which meant every combat mechanic the engine models was invisible to the agent and is the honest explanation for why a long run of real fidelity fixes produced only ±10pp eval noise. See `ASSUMPTIONS.md`.
- `train.py` sets `policy_kwargs=dict(net_arch=dict(pi=[256,256], vf=[256,256]))` — SB3's default `[64,64]` was narrower than the observation itself.
- **Reward is potential-based shaping PLUS two deliberately non-potential terms.** Not the `0.0` stub `ARCHITECTURE.md` describes, and no longer pure shaping either:
  `reward = SHAPING_GAMMA * Φ(s') - Φ(s) + turn_penalty - STRESS_TICK_PENALTY * stress_gained`, plus `REWARD_WIN`/`REWARD_LOSE` (`self._reward_win`/`_reward_lose` instance attrs, default `+5.0`/`-5.0`) on the terminal step. `SHAPING_GAMMA=0.99` must match PPO's `gamma` in `train.py` — that's what makes the *shaping* provably policy-invariant (Ng/Harada/Russell 1999). `Φ` = mean per-side score from `_hero_score`/`_enemy_score`/`_compute_potential`.
  - **Two-phase tempo model** (`is_recovery_phase()`): the per-turn cost is `TURN_PENALTY_THREAT=-0.10` normally, but drops to `TURN_PENALTY_STALL=-0.01` in the "recovery phase" — down to `STALL_PHASE_MAX_ENEMIES=1` living enemy, party actually needs healing (`_party_needs_recovery`), and under a per-combat `RECOVERY_TURN_BUDGET` of cheap turns. This encodes "know *when* stalling is worth it." All three gates matter; the budget is what actually bounds it, and dropping the need-gate produced a policy that dawdled forever at one enemy left (measured: 60% stall-outs with a healthy party). The old flat `-0.02` is what let the policy converge on turtling (Crusader healing on 73% of turns).
  - **`STRESS_TICK_PENALTY=0.01` per point of party stress *gained*, charged when it lands.** Only increases are charged — refunding stress healing would just pay the policy to farm heal-stress loops. This moved stress cost from terminal to dense; a sparse terminal penalty discounted across ~200 steps is worth ~13% of face value at decision time and could not discipline Abomination's Transform (free via `is_continue_turn`, +8 stress to every other party member, measured 1.77 uses per combat).
  - Because these two terms are non-potential, they **do change the optimal policy** — that is intentional, but it means the invariance argument only covers the `Φ` terms. Re-check `ASSUMPTIONS.md` before adding another one. See `tests/env/test_reward.py` for the backing tests (symmetry between attack/heal, no reward for standing still, etc.).
- **No built-in episode length cap** — `step()` never sets `truncated=True` on its own. `train.py`/`eval_expedition.py` both wrap the env in `gymnasium.wrappers.TimeLimit(max_episode_steps=150)`; if you construct `DarkestDungeonEnv`/`ExpeditionEnv` directly elsewhere, apply the same wrapper — a bad matchup can otherwise run thousands of steps (confirmed directly: an early training eval episode hit 3565 steps before this was added).
- **`render(mode="ansi")` now shows the action log, not just HP/position/stress.** `self._render_log_cursor` tracks how much of `self.engine.log` has already been shown; each call prints only the new lines (skill used, target, hit/miss/crit, buffs gained) since the last one, ahead of the state snapshot. Previously the only way to see what a policy actually did each turn was a one-off script reading `env.engine.log` directly; `train.py --play`'s existing `print(env.render())` loop now gets this for free. Robust to `self.engine.log` being reset out from under a live engine (`ExpeditionSequenceEnv` starting its next internal combat) via a length check that resets the cursor rather than silently dropping that combat's opening lines.

## Two of three training modes were dead, and nothing said so (2026-08-16)

`ActionMasker` was wired with `lambda e: e.action_masks()`. That relies on `gymnasium.Wrapper.__getattr__` forwarding an unknown attribute down to the inner env. **Gymnasium removed that forwarding in 1.x** — `hasattr(gymnasium.core.Wrapper, "__getattr__")` is `False` in 1.3.0 — so the callable raised `AttributeError: 'TimeLimit' object has no attribute 'action_masks'` on the first rollout step. Measured, by building each mode's real stack and calling `get_action_masks` the way `MaskablePPO.collect_rollouts` does:

| mode | env stack | before |
|---|---|---|
| `--full-expedition` | `ExpeditionSequenceEnv` | **works** |
| `--expedition` | `TimeLimit(ExpeditionEnv)` | `AttributeError` |
| default (fixed) | `TimeLimit(DarkestDungeonEnv)` | `AttributeError` |

Three things made it invisible. `requirements.txt` pinned `gymnasium>=1.0` with **no ceiling**, so it arrived as dependency drift and not as anyone's edit. The flagship mode is the one deliberately exempt from `TimeLimit` (it self-truncates), so the mode in daily use kept working. And **no test covered `train.py`'s wiring at all** — every engine test drives the raw env and never wraps it, so anything that only breaks once wrapped was untestable by construction.

Fix: one shared `env.gym_env.mask_fn` going through `.unwrapped`, replacing seven inline lambdas (`train`, `eval_expedition` ×2, `check_playbook`, `battle_report`, `analyze_behaviour`, `rollout`), so the next wrapper added anywhere cannot reintroduce this one site at a time. `.unwrapped` is correct for the unwrapped case too, since `gym.Env.unwrapped` returns `self`. `tests/training/test_train_wiring.py` pins all three modes; `requirements.txt` now bounds `gymnasium<2` and declares sb3/sb3-contrib/torch/pytest, which were installed but undeclared.

## Performance: the store, the skill scan, and the single env (2026-08-16)

Three independent costs, each measured before and after. None of them changes behaviour — the 807-test suite (now 839) is the guard rail, and every one of these landed green.

**`DataLoader.load_all` was uncached and rebuilt ~98× per suite run.** ~1.0 s each, so ~98 s of a 275 s baseline; `--collect-only` alone was 32 s from the ~29 module-level loads. `data/store_cache.py` memoises per process and to a 2.3 MB pickle (`dumps` 0.02 s, `loads` 0.03 s). **Invalidation is a stat-sweep digest** over every file the loader reads — the game subtrees, the CSV dirs, *and `data/*.py`*, since a parser edit changes the store without touching data. The sweep costs **0.172 s over 10,292 files**, ~6× cheaper than the rebuild it guards. A cache that can serve a stale store is not a trade-off in a project whose premise is that the game files are truth, which is why it is a digest and not a sentinel mtime. `DDSIM_NO_STORE_CACHE=1` bypasses, and the suite is green under it.

**A shared store legitimately grows.** `CombatEngine` memoises synthesised inline buffs into `store.buffs` under `__inline__*`/`__buff_type__*` (`combat_engine.py:2150`, `:2185`; `bridge/seed.py:350`). Once envs share a store that memo outlives them, so a store that has run combat holds more buffs than a fresh one — found as **2567 vs 2529**, and only under xdist, when another test in the same worker had run a fight first. It is get-or-create keyed on the effect name, and `store.effects` is name-keyed, so the id determines the definition and rewriting cannot change an answer. `tests/data/test_store_cache.py` exempts exactly those prefixes and fails on anything else, which is the tripwire for real pollution.

**`get_skill_data` scanned the whole game's skill catalogue by string id.** ~678 definitions (18 hero classes × ~7, 246 monsters × ~2), called **~15.6× per env step** from `step()`, `_execute_action()`, `get_valid_actions()` and `_skill_slot_vecs()`. Replaced by `DataStore.skill_index()` / `ally_skill_index()`, built lazily and pickled with the store. **Built with `setdefault` in the original scan order**, not plain assignment: the scan returned the FIRST match, so heroes beat monsters and an earlier duplicate beats a later one — assignment would make the last duplicate win and silently resolve a colliding id to a different skill's numbers. `tests/engine/test_skill_index.py` checks the index against the original algorithm, kept verbatim as an oracle, for every `(skill_id, level)` pair the game ships. Engine throughput **5851 → 7262 steps/s (+24%)**.

**Training was single-env on a 16-core machine.** `make_vec_env` adds `SubprocVecEnv` behind `--n-envs` (default 8). Rollout collection **766 → 5226 fps (6.8×)**. Each worker gets `sampler_seed + i` — with one seed everywhere, all N workers draw the same comp/enemy/torch sequence, giving N copies of one episode and a *narrower* distribution than the single env it replaced, while every throughput number still looked good. `worker_sampler_seeds` is split out as a pure function because with `SubprocVecEnv` the factories run inside the workers, where nothing in the parent can observe them (a monkeypatch-based test passed vacuously first).

**Two measured negatives, recorded so they are not retried:**

- **The GPU is slower.** One PPO iteration (24576 steps), 256×256 MLP: **CPU 17.1 s / 1433 fps, CUDA 24.7 s / 995 fps** on an RTX 2070. `batch_size=64` over a 24576-sample buffer is 3840 tiny kernel launches per iteration; the transfer never pays. `device="cpu"` stays.
- **Torch threads barely matter.** 1 → 4 → 8 threads: 17.5 s / 15.7 s / 16.6 s. SB3 is not starving the SGD (it leaves `torch.get_num_threads()` at 10); ~11% at best, not worth a knob.

**What parallelism does NOT fix.** With `n_envs=12` the SGD phase becomes ~73% of wall time, so end-to-end training improved only ~1.5× (60k steps: 88 s → 58 s) despite the 6.8× on rollout. Updates-per-timestep is invariant to `n_envs` at fixed `batch_size` — 0.156 either way — so the gradient work per sample is unchanged and only the rollout half parallelises. Going further means raising `batch_size` with the buffer, which trades sample-efficiency for wall-clock and **is an algorithm change, not a speed change**. Deliberately not done here: this project cannot currently distinguish a ~3pp effect from ~3pp noise, so it is exactly the kind of change that needs the A/B instrument first.

## The full-expedition eval was not reproducible, and that is why nothing ever resolved (2026-08-16)

`run_full_expedition_eval` builds its env as `ExpeditionSequenceEnv(sampler_seed=None)` — `random.Random(None)` seeds from OS entropy — and never reseeds `_sampler_rng` per episode. `reset(seed=)` only reseeds the COMBAT rng; which enemy comp each combat draws comes from the sampler. **So two runs of the identical command, on the identical checkpoint, face different monsters.** Measured, `--episodes 4 --comps 2 --comp-seed 1`, back to back:

| cell | run 1 | run 2 |
|---|---|---|
| Marked Prey: Snipe · medium | 75% | 50% |
| Marked Prey: Snipe · long | 25% | 0% |
| overall | **41.7%** | **33.3%** |

8.4pp of spread with the model held fixed. `bridge/rollout.py::Rollout.evaluate` has always done the reseed — its docstring spells out exactly why — but the eval path this project actually quotes never did. Every historical "this fix moved SUCCESS by 3pp", and the whole "~3pp spread between the two saves", was read off an instrument with more play in it than the effect.

`tools/ab_compare.py` fixes the ruler and then uses it:

- **Reseeds `_sampler_rng` from the scenario before every episode**, so a scenario is reproducible. `--self-check` plays one checkpoint as both arms and requires every paired delta to be exactly 0.0 across all 11 metrics; it passes bit-identically. Run it after touching the env, because a non-deterministic harness makes every number it prints meaningless.
- **Pairs the arms on one dungeon list.** Scenario difficulty is the dominant variance term and cancels exactly in a paired design.
- **Reports uncertainty**: percentile bootstrap over scenarios (not a t-interval — a per-scenario rate delta lives on {-1,0,1} and is nowhere near normal at these n), plus exact McNemar on the discordant pairs.

**First real use answered a question the project had been coin-flipping on.** Final save vs `logs/full_expedition/best_model.zip`, 216 paired scenarios:

| metric | best_model | final | delta | 95% CI | p |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SUCCESS | 29.2% | 37.5% | **+8.3pp** | [+1.9, +14.8] | 0.020 |
| completed | 31.0% | 40.3% | +9.3pp | [+2.3, +15.7] | 0.011 |
| retreats/expedition | 2.31 | 1.84 | -0.46 | [-0.76, -0.17] | — |
| final stress | 77.8 | 68.7 | -9.1 | [-15.7, -2.5] | — |
| reward | 37.5 | 45.1 | +7.6 | [+2.2, +13.2] | — |

162 of 216 tied; the signal is 54 discordant pairs. The final save is genuinely ahead — higher SUCCESS, fewer forced retreats, less terminal stress — so **commands defaulting to `best_model.zip` are defaulting to the worse checkpoint.**

The interval behaved as an instrument should: at 72 scenarios the same comparison read +9.7pp CI [-2.8, +22.2] (not distinguishable), at 216 it read +8.3pp CI [+1.9, +14.8]. Same point estimate, tighter interval. **±2pp would need ~2300 paired scenarios** — against archived evals of 36 cells. That is the honest answer to "training more doesn't improve the score": no eval this project ever ran could have detected the effect, in either direction.

Not fixed by this: *training* variance. Two runs at different `--seed` differ ~4pp on their own, which needs K seeds per arm and is a training question, not an eval one.

## Expedition training (`env/expedition_env.py`, `train.py --expedition`, `eval_expedition.py`)

`ExpeditionEnv(DarkestDungeonEnv)` randomizes (real hero comp, zone-matched real enemy encounter, paired Resolve Level) every `reset()` instead of the fixed default matchup — named after the game's own term for embarking a chosen party into a chosen zone. Goal: a policy that generalizes across comps/enemies instead of overfitting to one pairing. Built from two real data sources wired up for this: `store.comps` (`data/comps_csv_loader.py`, `COMPS/` CSV — was declared on `DataStore` but never actually loaded before this) and `store.enemy_comps` (see above). Sampling: pick a random hero comp whose `Location` (Ruins/Warrens/Cove/Weald) maps to a trainable zone, then weighted-pick (by real `.chance`) a `hall`/`room`/`boss`/`stall` enemy group from that *same* zone — `named` (scripted) encounters and non-zone-mapped comps (`DD2`, `Courtyard`) are excluded. Confirmed via real data that mash file tiers 1-2/3-4/5 are exactly Apprentice/Veteran/Champion (by enemy HP scaling), so `TIER_TO_HERO_LEVEL` fields the party at the top of the matching bracket — resolve 2/4/6 — instead of always max-gear heroes facing the full unpaired difficulty range (user-specified: a level-2/4/6 party is equipped at weapon/armour/skill tier 2/4/5(max) via the `tier_idx = level-1` mapping above). `eval_expedition.py` evaluates a trained model the same way — across difficulty bands × several sampled comps, many episodes each, deterministic policy, same level pairing. Each sampled comp is now also restricted to its real 4-skill-per-hero loadout when one exists (`store.comp_loadouts`, see below) instead of every hero's full ~7-skill movepool.

Found and fixed while building this: `CombatEngine.reset()` assigned enemy positions via naive `enumerate()` (`position=i`), not accounting for a size-2 enemy occupying 2 ranks — ~25% of real multi-enemy encounter groups combine a size-2 enemy (e.g. `skeleton_captain_A`, `brigand_blood_A`) with others, so the next enemy would silently land on the size-2 enemy's second occupied position. Fixed via a running slot cursor advanced by each enemy's own `.size` instead of the list index (verified: no real comp's total size-units exceed the 4-slot board).

## Full-expedition simulation (`env/expedition_sequence_env.py`, `train.py --full-expedition`, `eval_expedition.py --full-expedition`)

`ExpeditionSequenceEnv(ExpeditionEnv)` — one Gym episode = a full 8-18 combat expedition (real comp/zone sampled once, one Resolve-Level-paired difficulty tier fixed for the whole trip via `TIER_TO_HERO_LEVEL`, per-combat enemy encounter re-sampled from that same zone/tier each fight), instead of `ExpeditionEnv`'s single randomized combat per episode. Both env classes stay live — `ExpeditionEnv` is unchanged and still useful for single-combat training/eval. Chains `CombatEngine.reset()` calls internally via `_start_next_combat()`; observation space extends `DarkestDungeonEnv`'s 324 floats to 331 (`EXPEDITION_CONTEXT_FEATURES = 7` expedition-context floats: combats-remaining fraction, alive-hero fraction, mean living-party stress fraction, 4× per-provision-type remaining fraction). Self-truncates at `MAX_STEPS_PER_COMBAT(40) * num_combats` inside `step()` instead of being wrapped in the external `gymnasium.wrappers.TimeLimit` (the right cap depends on the sampled expedition length, unknown until `reset()`).

- **Expedition length**: `EXPEDITION_LENGTH_BANDS = {"short": (8,11), "medium": (10,14), "long": (14,18)}` — user-specified; no in-game data file exposes real per-length combat/room counts (dungeon layout generation is hardcoded in the game binary, confirmed via exhaustive search), same "simulator convention" treatment as `TIER_TO_HERO_LEVEL`.
