- **Cross-combat persistence**: HP/stress/Death's-Door/mortality-tier carry between combats via `CombatEngine.reset(carry_over=...)` (`CarryOverHeroState`, `engine/combatant.py`) — everything else (buffs, DoTs, guard, riposte, stun, cooldowns, round, `is_afflicted`/`is_virtuous`) resets fresh every combat, matching the real game (affliction is a per-battle mental state; skill buffs/debuffs expire in the corridor after combat).
- **Hero death mid-expedition does not end the episode** — the party continues with survivors. Dropping a dead hero from `hero_defs` and leaving `hero_positions` unset naturally re-packs the rest into contiguous ranks 0..N-1 for the next combat (the "reposition after a hero is lost" the user asked for, achieved for free by the existing default-positions behavior). Only a full party wipe or the last sampled combat's end truly ends the episode.
- **Hero-death position collapse WITHIN a combat** (`_collapse_hero_position_gap`, `engine/combat_engine.py`): a real, previously-missing mechanic found while implementing this — heroes never leave a corpse (unlike enemies, whose `is_corpse` flag blocks their rank until cleared), so everyone behind a hero who just died should immediately shift one rank toward the front. Wired into `_handle_deaths_door`'s hero-death branch.
- **Pass-turn fallback** (`__pass__` action, `PASS_TURN_STRESS = 5.0`, no `rules.json` source — simulator convention matching `stall_stress`'s own +5/round): another real gap found alongside the above — a hero with truly zero legal actions (boxed in, no reachable skill or Move target) had no engine-level fallback; the raw `CombatEngine` would hang forever waiting for `submit_action()`, only the Gym wrapper's index-clamping trick silently no-op'd it (no stress cost, no log entry). Now `get_valid_actions` offers `__pass__` only when nothing else is legal, and using it costs stress.
- **Provisions** (`data/provision_loader.py`, `store.provisions`/`store.hero_class_starting_provisions`): bandage (cures Bleed), antivenom (cures Blight), holy_water (33% resist buff to blight/bleed/disease/debuff, 3 rounds — its real `.buff_ids` line is an *unquoted* multi-value bareword list, which `EffectLoader` mishandled as one un-splittable string before this, silently no-op'ing every such effect project-wide, including `leper_*`/`medicinal_herbs_*`/Abomination's `rake_buff_*l`/`*h` — fixed by falling back to `str(raw_ids).split()`), laudanum (clears Horror, a real stress-damage-over-time status effect — e.g. Ghoul's `howl`, Madman's `accusation`; see "Torchlight, monster AI..." below for why this was previously believed inert) are free (turn-preserving) in-combat actions (`__provision_<id>__`) — `CombatEngine.step()` skips the turn-advance/DoT-tick for these so the same hero is re-offered immediately (bounded by finite inventory). Food is not an in-combat action (no real combat effect record exists for it) — handled at the expedition layer instead as a between-combat 5% max-HP heal (`FOOD_HEAL_FRACTION`, real `rules.json` `provision_hp_heal`), auto-consumed once per combat transition while supply lasts. Starting quantities (`BASE_PROVISION_AMOUNTS`/`LENGTH_BAND_PROVISION_MULT`/`FOOD_AMOUNTS` in `expedition_sequence_env.py`) are an explicitly-flagged simulator convention — no real "recommended loadout" data exists (`campaign/provision/provision.json` only has store purchase *limits* per dungeon-length index) — capped at each item's real stack limit; each hero's real free class-specific starting item (`raid_starting_hero_class_item_lists`, e.g. crusader→+1 holy_water) is added on top for whichever of the 3 in-scope items apply.
- **Reinforcements** (`_spawn_reinforcements`, `_current_enemy_tier_suffix`, `engine/combat_engine.py`): the real, fixed, zone-independent `stall_summon_A/B/C` effect (always 2x `cultist_brawler` + `cultist_witch`, tier-matched to the fight's own A/B/C by majority vote) fires once when `stall_counter` reaches `rules.stall_round_summon_effect_threshold` (4) — this rules.json field was already loaded but never consulted anywhere before this. Spawned onto open ranks (front-most if the monster's real `wave_spawning.prefers_front` flag is set — verified: `cultist_brawler` has it, `cultist_witch` doesn't).
- **`_check_stall`'s core condition was backwards from the real mechanic — found chasing why "Stun Control" comps' fights kept dragging on/wiping even after the heal-effect and group-targeting fixes.** It used to increment `stall_counter` purely from `alive_enemies <= stall_enemy_size_threshold`, with no other condition at all. The real, wiki-confirmed rule (and `rules.json` has the fields to prove it — 2 of which were loaded by nobody until now) is: a round only counts as a stall if the party ALSO failed to land `stall_number_of_invalidating_skills_threshold` (2) "real" actions (`SkillLevelData.is_stall_invalidating`, default `True` — heals/buffs/utility skills explicitly set it `False`) **and** the party isn't nearly wiped (`alive_heroes >= stall_party_size_threshold`, 3). Previously, any comp that naturally grinds a fight down to its last 1-2 enemies over several rounds — precisely what a Stun/Blight-control comp is *designed* to do — kept accruing stall regardless of how aggressively it was actually attacking, spawning bogus reinforcement waves and prolonging fights past anything real play would see. Confirmed via replay: 1-4 reinforcement spawns within a single 8-combat "Stun Control: Blight" episode, correlating with combats stalling out near the internal per-combat step cap instead of resolving. Fixed: new `CombatEngine._stall_invalidating_actions_this_round` counter, incremented in `_track_skill_use` (hero actions only) and reset each round in `_check_stall`, which now gates on all three real conditions (also switched from a one-step decay to a full reset on a non-stall round, per the wiki's own "the timer resets" description). `stall_party_size_threshold`/`stall_number_of_invalidating_skills_threshold` added to `CombatRules`/`RulesLoader.KEY_MAP`.
- **Accelerated/disabled stalling** (`EnemyDefinition.accelerate_stall_penalty`/`disable_stall_penalty`, the real `battle_modifier:` record): looked up on `darkestdungeon.wiki.gg/wiki/Stalling` to pin down the two `rules.json` fields left unresolved by the fix above. Confirmed: when **every** living enemy has `.accelerate_stall_penalty` (Bone Arbalist/Courtier/Rabble/Soldier, Carrion Eater, Crone, Fungal Artillery, Madman, Pelagic Guardian/Shaman, Swine Drummer/Skiver, Webber, and others — independently corroborated against `HEROES/Heroes/Occultist *.md`'s own "enemies that accelerate reinforcements" list), reinforcements arrive after 2 stall rounds instead of 4, and the round-3 stress penalty never applies at all. When **any** living enemy has `.disable_stall_penalty` (~119 monsters — most boss/Ancestor forms, `brigand_blood` at every tier, etc.), the fight is exempt from stalling entirely. Both flags were already correctly parseable by the darkest parser (the first is even name-dropped in `data/darkest_parser.py`'s own comments from an earlier boolean-capitalization fix) but were never read into `EnemyDefinition` or consulted anywhere. `stall_round_accelerated_only_increment`/`_consecutive_increment` (the two `rules.json` fields presumably driving the exact accelerated-increment arithmetic) still aren't consulted — the wiki-confirmed *outcome* (threshold 2, no stress) is applied directly via a new `ACCELERATED_STALL_SUMMON_THRESHOLD` simulator-convention constant instead, since the precise formula those two fields encode couldn't be pinned down with confidence.
- **A handful of AoE attack skills fire a party-wide effect once per enemy hit instead of once per use — found auditing Arbalest for the same session's investigation.** Some AoE attacks against the enemy side carry ONE effect that redirects to the whole ALLY side via its own `.target` (`"performer_group_other"`/`"performer_group"` — the same group-dispatch mechanism fixed earlier this session) — e.g. Arbalest/Musketeer's Rallying Flare (`flare`), whose `"FlareHealStress N"` is a small party stress-relief bundled alongside its per-enemy Destealth. Since `_execute_action`'s attack branch calls `_apply_skill_effects` once per AoE sub-target *hit*, this group effect re-fired once per enemy hit — confirmed via direct engine test against 3 living enemies: a single Rallying Flare use healed anywhere from 0 to 12 stress depending on hit RNG and `FlareHealStress`'s own chance roll, instead of the flat "small Stress heal" the real skill is (`HEROES/Heroes/Arbalest *.md`). Fixed generally (not just for this one skill) via new `_split_group_effect_names(skill)`, applied uniformly across all 3 multi-target branches (`is_heal`, ally-skill, attack): group-targeted effects are pulled out of the per-target loop and applied exactly once per action (gated on whether *any* target was hit/the skill was used, matching the existing `_apply_self_effects` "once per action" convention already used for self-buffs).
- **Riposte counter-attacks were guaranteed to hit — no accuracy roll existed at all — found auditing Man-at-Arms, whose own doc calls this out explicitly: "Riposte's ACC stays fixed at 90 and DOES NOT scale with Retribution's Skill Level... IS affected by ACC modifiers such as quirks, trinkets, Buffs/Debuffs."** `_maybe_trigger_riposte`'s `chance`/`on_hit_chance`/`on_miss_chance` roll only ever gated whether a counter-attack was *attempted*; once triggered, damage applied unconditionally. The real counter-attack has its own fixed base ACC/crit, from a data record this project had never even looked for: `riposte_skill:` (distinct from `combat_skill:` — Man-at-Arms 90%/+0% crit, Bounty Hunter/Highwayman 85%/+5% crit each, verified present for exactly these 3 real riposte-capable classes and no others). This directly affects the two best-performing comps this whole session ("Riposte Style (Hag)", "Holy Hymn: Riposte") — their core mechanic was, until this fix, literally unmissable. Fixed: `HeroDefinition` gained `riposte_acc`/`riposte_crit_mod: Optional[float]`, parsed from `riposte_skill:` in `HeroLoader._build_hero`; `_maybe_trigger_riposte` now rolls a real hit chance (`riposte_acc + defender.atk_bonus - attacker.dodge`, clamped by the same `cutoff_always_miss`/`cutoff_always_hit` every other attack uses) before applying damage, and adds `riposte_crit_mod` on top of the setup-effect's own `crit_mod` for the crit roll (two independent, additive real crit sources — analogous to how a normal skill's crit is `attacker.crit + skill.crit`). Falls back to the old guaranteed-hit behavior only when the defender has no `riposte_acc` data (the rare monster-side riposte case — 1 real monster file has `riposte_skill:`, out of scope here). This is expected to reduce, not increase, Riposte-comp zero-death rates in the next retrain — a case where more fidelity means a *harder* fight, not an easier one, and the honest number is what matters.
- **`SkillLevelData.is_crit_valid` was parsed but never consulted anywhere — every skill could crit regardless of what the real data says.** Found auditing Vestal, whose doc flags Illumination as "CANNOT CRIT" — checked `resolution.py`'s `check_crit` and confirmed it only ever computed `attacker.crit + skill.crit`, with no reference to `skill.is_crit_valid` (default `True`; real data explicitly sets it `False` on 65 skill-level records across 10 hero files — Plague Doctor's `blinding_gas`/`disorienting_blast`, Arbalest/Musketeer's `flare`, Vestal's `illumination`, and others). A crit-ineligible skill crit-ing incorrectly triggers stress relief (`_stress_relief_performer`) and per-class on-crit passives (`_trigger_on_crit_passive`) — directly relevant *again* to the "Stun Control" comps, whose signature Plague Doctor CC skills are two of the affected ones. Fixed: `check_crit` returns `False` immediately when `not skill.is_crit_valid`, before rolling. Verified both directions in one test: a forced 100%-crit-chance `blinding_gas` never crits (and never relieves stress), while `manacles` (no override) under the identical setup still does.
- **A Stunned defender's Riposte never gets suppressed** — found reading Highwayman's doc (the other real riposte class) right after the ACC fix above, which independently confirms the same `riposte_skill:` data (Highwayman 85%/+5% crit, matching exactly) and separately states "Ripostes aren't activated when Stunned" / a trinket's Stun Resist downside is called out as "crippling for Highwayman, as Ripostes require avoiding Stuns." `_maybe_trigger_riposte` never checked `defender.is_stunned` at all — Riposte is purely reactive (fires off *being attacked*, not off the defender's own turn), so nothing else in the engine incidentally covered this the way the stun-skip in `_advance_to_next_hero_turn` already covers a stunned combatant's own turn. Fixed with one added condition in the existing early-return guard.
- **Every single attack roll in the simulator was missing a universal hidden +5 ACC — the broadest-impact fix of the whole session, found reading Houndmaster's doc.** His doc explains why Guard Dog can't reach true 100% dodge immunity: *"A hidden +5 ACC modifier always applies to any hero/enemy attack so an attack always has a minimum 5% chance to hit, regardless of the target's DODGE."* Cross-checked with a web search on the mechanic, which independently corroborates the exact real formula: `ACC + 5 - DODGE = Hit Chance`. `resolution.py`'s `compute_hit_chance` computed a bare `acc - dodge` with no `+5` anywhere — confirmed this is genuinely missing, not just differently named: `rules.json`'s `cutoffAlwaysMiss` is `0.00` (a real, separate, already-correctly-used safety clamp preventing a *negative* hit chance in extreme edge cases), not a 5%-floor mechanic — the `+5` is what actually produces the practical minimum players experience in normal play, and it's not backed by any `rules.json` field at all (same "hardcoded in the binary" treatment as `TIER_TO_HERO_LEVEL`/the heart-attack threshold). Fixed: new `HIDDEN_ACC_BONUS = 0.05` constant in `resolution.py`, added inside `compute_hit_chance`'s formula (before the `cutoff_always_miss`/`cutoff_always_hit` clamp, so the clamp behavior itself is unchanged) and mirrored into `_maybe_trigger_riposte`'s own hand-rolled hit-chance formula for consistency (Riposte doesn't route through `compute_hit_chance` since it has no full `SkillLevelData` to build one from). **4 pre-existing tests needed correcting, not just extending** — `test_carry_over_reset.py`'s and `test_heart_attack.py`'s "fresh hero" helpers used Crusader (SPD 3, the slowest hero in the game) against a faster enemy, relying on `reset()`'s pre-hero-turn auto-resolution (see `CombatEngine.reset()`'s docstring) never landing a hit at their fixed seed — a coincidence of the old, less-accurate hit-chance formula, not a real invariant; switched both to a fast hero (Jester, SPD 9) so no enemy ever acts first. `test_resolution.py`'s direct `compute_hit_chance` unit test had its expected value corrected (25%→30% for 50% ACC vs 25% dodge) and gained a new dedicated test for the bonus itself.
- **A second, much broader instance of the Rallying Flare AoE-effect bug — found by checking every real skill for the same shape, not just the one already fixed.** `_split_group_effect_names`'s fix above only pulled out effects targeting a whole *group* (`"performer_group_other"`/`"performer_group"`); a `target: "performer"` (plain self) effect nested in an AoE attack was still applied once per AoE sub-target hit, same underlying bug, different target shape. Checked every hero/monster AoE skill in the whole install for a `target=="performer"` effect in its list: **57 real cases**, and — checked directly — every single one already carries the real `.apply_once true` field (`EffectDefinition.is_apply_once`, parsed since an earlier session but never once consulted anywhere in `engine/`). Concrete, high-impact examples found this way: Hellion's own signature `barbaric_yawp` (her double-frontline-stun) and `breakthru` would double/triple-stack her "Hellion Exhaust" self-debuff depending on how many of the 2-3 AoE targets were hit; Jester's `battle_ballad`/`harvest`/`solo` would over-charge her "Build to Finale" stacking counter toward Finale; Occultist's `abyssal_artillery` would double-stack "Abyssal Killer"; several monster AoE skills (Shambler's tentacle summons, Necromancer's reinforcement summons) would spawn extra unintended summons. Fixed by widening `_split_group_effect_names`'s condition to `eff.target in group_specs OR eff.is_apply_once` — both signals independently justify pulling an effect out of the per-target loop into the once-per-action bucket, and they're not mutually exclusive (`"FlareHealStress N"` has both; `beast_stress_party` has only the group-target signal; `Hellion Exhaust` has only `apply_once`). Verified directly: Barbaric YAWP against 2 enemies applies `HellionExSPD`'s real `-3` speed delta exactly once, not `-6`.
- **Move action** (`combat_move_skill:` per-hero record, `data/hero_loader.py`/`MoveSkillData`): a real, previously-unparsed per-hero voluntary reposition ability (`.move <backward> <forward>`, verified varies genuinely by class — e.g. Arbalest/Musketeer 2 back/0 forward, Jester 3/3, Flagellant 1/3). Wired as a genuine turn-costing action (`__move__`, unlike provisions) in `get_valid_actions`/`_execute_action`, gated by the hero's real `.launch` positions and unable to move onto a rank a living ally occupies.
- **Abomination fidelity fixes** (found via a `HEROES/Heroes/Abomination *.md` doc audit, prompted by "the model doesn't seem to be portraying Abomination well"): four real, confirmed gaps, all now fixed.
  1. **He was being skill-restricted like every other hero.** The doc is explicit: "Unlike other heroes who can only equip 4 combat skills, Abomination always has all 7 combat skills equipped" — there's no 4-skill loadout choice for him at all. But the scraped `COMPS/Comps/<name>/<name>.json` (see "Real per-comp skill loadouts" above) still lists exactly 4 `activeSkills` for him, same as any other hero (the source tool has no Abomination carve-out) — confirmed directly on "Stun Control: Beast"'s 2nd Abomination, whose JSON list (`transform`/`manacles`/`vomit`/`absolution`) is missing **all three** of his beast-form attacks (`rake`/`rage`/`slam`), which would leave him unable to deal any damage at all while transformed if applied as a real restriction. Fixed in `ExpeditionEnv._resolve_skill_ids`/`eval_expedition.py`'s `resolve_skill_ids`: any hero whose `hero_id == "abomination"` always resolves to `[]` (unrestricted — `CombatEngine.reset()`'s `if ids:` check already treats an empty per-hero entry as "use the full movepool," so no engine change was needed), while the rest of the comp's heroes still resolve their real 4-skill loadouts normally.
  2. **Transform's `.is_continue_turn` (real data: "FREE ACTION, does not end your turn" per the wiki, letting Abomination transform AND attack in the same turn) was parsed into `SkillLevelData` but never consulted anywhere in the engine.** Every Transform use silently cost the hero's entire turn like any other skill — a severe behavioral gap for a mechanic the game explicitly calls out as free, and a plausible direct contributor to why Transform-dependent comps (the three "Stun Control" comps, all Abomination-heavy) underperformed: the policy could never combo transform-into-Beast with a same-turn attack the way a real player would. Fixed in `CombatEngine.step()` — `is_free_action` (previously provision-only) now also covers any skill with `skill_data.is_continue_turn`, skipping the turn-advance/DoT-tick exactly like a provision use. Verified project-wide: `is_continue_turn true` appears in exactly one file in the whole game install (`heroes/abomination/abomination.info.darkest`, Transform only), so this fix is correctly scoped to just that skill.
  3. **`get_valid_actions` never filtered by `per_battle_limit`/`per_turn_limit`** (Transform: 2/battle, 1/turn) — only execution-time `_check_skill_limits` did. A maxed-out Transform was still offered as a legal action, silently rejected on selection (wasting the whole turn) — the exact same pathology class as the mode-filter bug fixed earlier (`_is_valid_mode`/`_check_valid_mode`). Fixed the same way: new side-effect-free `_has_skill_uses_remaining` (shared by both `get_valid_actions`' filter and `_check_skill_limits`'s execution-time check, which keeps its log line).
  4. **Affliction-triggered forced mode revert was unimplemented.** Real data: only Abomination's `beast` mode record sets `.affliction_combat_skill_id "transform"` — becoming Afflicted while in Beast form force-transforms him back to Human for free, distinct from a player-chosen Transform and *not* counted against his 2-per-battle limit. Without this, an Abomination who'd already spent both charges and then got Afflicted in Beast form would be stuck there (Rake/Rage/Slam only) for the rest of the fight, unlike the real game. Fixed via new `_maybe_force_mode_revert`, called from `_apply_affliction_effects` — looks up the hero's current-mode `HeroMode` record, and if it has `affliction_combat_skill_id` set, resolves that skill's `human_effects`/`beast_effects` (same mechanism `_resolve_self_action` uses for a normal Transform) without calling `_track_skill_use`. `battle_complete_combat_skill_id` (the same mode record's other auto-revert trigger, on battle end) is deliberately left unimplemented — inert given `CombatEngine.reset()` already starts every new combat at a fresh default mode regardless of how the previous combat ended, so there's no behavior for it to change.
  Tests: `tests/env/test_expedition_env.py::test_abomination_is_never_skill_restricted`, `tests/engine/test_transform.py::test_transform_is_free_action_does_not_advance_turn`/`test_transform_not_offered_once_per_battle_limit_exhausted`/`test_affliction_in_beast_mode_forces_revert_to_human` (plus `test_transform_switches_mode_both_directions` updated — its second Transform call now needs an explicit round advance, since a free action correctly no longer auto-advances the round the old, buggy version relied on to dodge `per_turn_limit`).
- **Per-class on-crit passives** (`crit: .effects "on_crit_X"` per-hero record): fires whenever a hero lands ANY crit with ANY skill (including riposte crits) — separate from a skill's own `.effect` list. All 18 catalogued (e.g. Crusader/Shieldbreaker→+15% PROT/2rd, Highwayman→+speed, Abomination→+20% DMG). Found and fixed a related generic bug while wiring this: `_resolve_effect`'s `.buff_ids` loop only tracked a buff for later reversal when the *effect* record itself had `.duration` — several on-crit effects have no effect-level duration even though their buff does, so the fix falls back to the buff definition's own duration. Also added the previously-missing `poison_chance`/`bleed_chance` stat_types (`CombatantState.blight_chance_bonus`/`bleed_chance_bonus`, reduces the target's effective resist floor in `_resolve_effect`'s bleed/blight rolls) and party-wide crit stress/relief (`_apply_stress_to_allies`, wired into both the enemy-crit-vs-hero and hero-crit-self-relief paths via previously-unread `rules.json` `monster_crit_companion_*`/`player_crit_companion_*` fields).
- **3-tier Death's Door** (`deaths_door:` record's `.recovery_buffs`/`.recovery_heart_attack_buffs`, previously only `.buffs` — the active tier — was read): a sticky, monotonic `CombatantState.mortality_tier` (`"none"→"mortality"→"heart_attack"`, never downgrades — confirmed via real buff amounts: mortality's stress term is +10%, heart_attack's is **-15%**, opposite signs rule out additive stacking). The weaker "mortality" tier applies for the rest of the expedition the first time a hero is healed back up after Death's Door; a stress-200 heart attack (below) instead applies the separate "heart_attack" tier.
- **Heart attack** (`HEART_ATTACK_STRESS_THRESHOLD=200`, `HEART_ATTACK_RESULTING_STRESS=170`, wiki-sourced — no `rules.json` key exposes this, same "hardcoded in the binary" treatment as `TIER_TO_HERO_LEVEL`): an Afflicted hero whose stress reaches 200 suffers HP→0/Death's Door (stress reset to 170) or instant death (bypassing the deathblow-resist roll) if already at Death's Door. A Virtuous hero at 200 loses Virtue and resets to 0 instead.
- **Reward — realigned with the real objectives** (`env/expedition_sequence_env.py`'s `step()`): the original design (`REWARD_WIN=+5.0`/`REWARD_LOSE=-5.0` per internal combat, `REWARD_HERO_DEATH=-3.0`, stress purely potential-based) trained to 3M then 6M timesteps and plateaued at 0-8% zero-death rate with final stress pinned near the 200 heart-attack ceiling almost everywhere — diagnosis: summed over an 8-18 combat expedition, `REWARD_WIN` alone reaches +90, dwarfing a -3 death penalty, and the stress potential telescopes to ~0 over the episode (Ng/Harada/Russell) so it never entered the objective at all, only reshaped learning dynamics. Worse, `Φ_expedition` averaged over *living* heroes only, so a stressed hero's dying dropped their high-stress term out of the mean — a real spurious positive-reward gradient rewarding killing off stressed heroes, not just a tuning issue. Fixed by making the real (non-potential, optimum-changing) terms dominate: `DarkestDungeonEnv.__init__` now sets `self._reward_win`/`self._reward_lose` as instance attrs (default = the unchanged module constants for `DarkestDungeonEnv`/`ExpeditionEnv`), and `ExpeditionSequenceEnv.__init__` overrides `self._reward_win` (currently `8.0`) while leaving `_reward_lose` at the inherited default. `REWARD_HERO_DEATH` raised to `-20.0` (now outweighs the win reward from a whole expedition of combats). Three **real, non-potential** terminal terms in `step()`: `REWARD_EXPEDITION_COMPLETE=+10.0` fires once, only at the true expedition end (`terminated and not party_wiped` — this can only mean the last combat was won, since `CombatEngine._check_win_loss` sets `winner=="enemies"` iff no heroes are alive), never on an internal combat transition; `REWARD_TRUNCATION=-20.0` on a stall-out, so running out the step budget is never a cheap way to escape a bad expedition; `TERMINAL_STRESS_PENALTY_WEIGHT=4.0` subtracts `4 * mean_roster_stress_frac` at any true end (`terminated or truncated`), putting final-stress minimization directly in the objective instead of leaving it shaping-only. **These four magnitudes are actively tuned — read them from `env/expedition_sequence_env.py`, not from this file.** `TERMINAL_STRESS_PENALTY_WEIGHT` in particular was cut from `10.0` to `4.0` when `STRESS_TICK_PENALTY` moved most of the stress cost from terminal to dense; the two are meant to be changed together. `_compute_expedition_potential`/`_mean_roster_stress_frac` (the latter factored out, shared by both) now average over the **original roster**, not just the living — heroes dead in a prior combat (absent from `engine.heroes`) or `is_dead` in the current one count as max stress (frac `1.0`), which is always `>=` any living stress fraction, closing the perverse gradient (death can now only ever make roster-mean stress non-decreasing). The pre-existing per-combat Φ (`DarkestDungeonEnv.step()`, reused unchanged via `super().step()`) and `Φ_expedition` (same `SHAPING_GAMMA`, still forced to 0 at any true terminal step) are kept as-is — both are policy-invariant (Ng/Harada/Russell 1999 composes linearly across summed potential-based terms) and were never the misalignment; only the real terms needed to change. All magnitudes (`_reward_win=1.0`, `REWARD_HERO_DEATH=-20.0`, `REWARD_EXPEDITION_COMPLETE=10.0`, `TERMINAL_STRESS_PENALTY_WEIGHT=10.0`) are simulator convention/starting points, not derived — no game data exposes "how much an RL reward should penalize a death." The pre-realignment 6M-timestep model (`dd_combat_ppo_full_expedition.zip`) is obsolete under the new reward (can't `--resume` across a reward change) and needs a from-scratch retrain; see `ASSUMPTIONS.md` for the full before/after eval tables and design rationale.

## Wiki-driven fidelity fixes, round 2 (`WIKI_MECHANICS.md` extraction, 2026-08-07)

Three confirmed gaps from a token-efficient extract of `darkestdungeon.wiki.gg`'s Gameplay category — see `WIKI_MECHANICS.md` for the extract itself and the full "likely, not yet code-verified" list this pass didn't chase.

- **PROT had no 80% cap.** Wiki-confirmed: "PROT is capped at 80% — excess has no effect." `resolution.py`'s `resolve_attack` applied `(1.0 - target.prot)` directly with no ceiling, while the *stat itself* (a different quantity) is clamped to 0.95 by `_apply_buff_to_stats`/`_reverse_buff_from_stats` (`combat_engine.py:376`/`468`) — so any combatant stacked past 80% PROT (Stalwart virtue +15%, Bulwark, guard buffs, trinkets) took less damage than the real game allows, and could reach as low as 5% incoming damage instead of the real 20% floor. No `rules.json` field backs the cap — grepped the whole shipped file for any `prot`-named key and found none, same "hardcoded in the binary" situation as `HIDDEN_ACC_BONUS`. Fixed: new `PROT_DAMAGE_REDUCTION_CAP = 0.80` in `resolution.py`, applied as `min(target.prot, PROT_DAMAGE_REDUCTION_CAP)` right before the damage multiply (the *stat* stays uncapped at 0.95 — only its damage effect is capped, matching the wiki's own framing of "excess has no effect" rather than "excess is impossible"). Test: `tests/engine/test_resolution.py::test_prot_is_capped_at_80_percent` (0.95 PROT still takes 20% of base damage, not 5%).
- **Restoration (heal-over-time) ticked after Bleed/Blight instead of before.** Wiki-confirmed ordering (`Status_Effects`). `_advance_to_next_hero_turn`'s start-of-turn tick block ran `_tick_dots` → `_tick_stress_dots` → `_tick_heal_dots`; reordered to `_tick_heal_dots` → `_tick_dots` → `_tick_stress_dots`. Fixing the order alone surfaced a second, previously-latent bug: `_apply_heal`'s Death's-Door recovery check (`if target.hp > 0 and target.is_deaths_door: ...recover...`) used to fire *inline*, immediately after adding the heal amount — correct for a normal mid-combat heal (a single isolated HP change), but wrong once a heal_dot and a bleed/blight dot both tick in the same start-of-turn phase: a heal_dot alone crossing above 0 HP could flip a hero off Death's Door before that same turn's bleed/blight had even been subtracted, and then `_check_deaths_door_dot` (called once at the very end, seeing the post-bleed negative HP) would treat it as a **fresh** Death's-Door entry — re-applying entry debuffs/stress and, critically, **skipping the deathblow roll** a hero already on Death's Door owes for taking net-negative damage that turn (`_handle_deaths_door`'s `else` branch, which only fires when `is_deaths_door` was never cleared). Concretely reachable, not hypothetical: Flagellant's Reclaim is exactly a self-heal-dot, and several of her other skills self-inflict Bleed — a Flagellant recovering via Reclaim while self-bleeding is the intended use case for this exact interaction. Fixed by decoupling the HP mutation from the recovery side-effect: `_apply_heal` gained `check_recovery: bool = True`; `_tick_heal_dots` now calls it with `check_recovery=False`, and the turn-start tick block does a single `if combatant.hp > 0 and combatant.is_deaths_door: self._recover_from_deaths_door(combatant)` reconciliation *after* all three ticks (heal, bleed/blight, stress) have applied — mirroring how entry-into-Death's-Door was already centralized in `_check_deaths_door_dot`, just extended to cover the exit direction too. The extracted `_recover_from_deaths_door` helper is unchanged behavior for every other `_apply_heal` caller (direct skill heals, provisions, monster self-heals) — none of those pass `check_recovery=False`, so the inline-check behavior there is identical to before. Tests: `tests/engine/test_gaps.py::test_heal_dot_ticks_before_bleed_blight_each_turn` (order), `::test_heal_dot_does_not_cause_premature_deaths_door_recovery` (the reconciliation fix — net -2 HP for the turn, hero must never be logged as recovering).
- **Initiative tie-breaks on equal SPD+d8 were correct only by accident.** Wiki-confirmed rule: heroes act before enemies, lower rank first, on a tie. `_compute_turn_order`'s sort key was `(1 if is_stunned else 0, -(spd+roll))` — no tie-break term at all. It happened to produce the right order because `combatants = [c for c in self.heroes + self.enemies if ...]` builds the pre-sort list heroes-first, and both `self.heroes`/`self.enemies` are already in rank order (`position = index` at `reset()`), and Python's `list.sort()` is stable — three separate implementation facts none of which were documented or tested, any one of which (a future `sorted()` swap, reordering the concat, an unstable-sort refactor) would have silently broken it with no test failure. Fixed by making the tie-break an explicit fourth key: `(1 if is_stunned else 0, -(spd+roll), 0 if is_hero else 1, position)`. Test: `tests/engine/test_gaps.py::test_initiative_tie_break_is_explicit_heroes_first_then_lower_rank` (forces a tie by overriding `engine.rng.roll` to a constant and setting all four combatants' speed equal, asserts the exact resulting order).
- **Correction to the extract itself, found while fixing the above, not a code change:** `WIKI_MECHANICS.md`'s first draft flagged "crit doesn't extend DoT duration/application chance" as an unverified likely gap. It's wrong — `tests/engine/test_gaps.py::test_crit_extends_dot_duration_and_status_chance` already covers the real +50%-duration-rounded-up behavior via `_resolve_effect`. Corrected in the extract rather than silently deleted, so the wrong claim doesn't get re-asserted later.

## Wiki-driven fidelity fixes, round 3 — the remaining "likely gaps" list, checked and closed (2026-08-07)

Round 2 above left 7 items on an unverified "likely gap" list. Each was checked directly against the code (empirically where possible, not just read) before touching anything — 2 turned out already correct, 1 was moot by construction, 1 was an already-documented scope boundary, and 3 were real, now fixed. See `WIKI_MECHANICS.md` for the condensed per-item writeup; this entry is the full evidence trail.

- **Crit-heal was entirely unmodeled — the highest-impact of the three.** No heal skill, self-heal, named-effect heal, or heal-dot tick ever crit, regardless of `attacker.crit`/`skill.crit`, because none of those paths route through `resolve_attack` (the only place a crit roll happened). Wiki-confirmed via a direct fetch of `Critical_Hit`: "Critical heals: fixed rate — 12% for single-target heals, 5% per target for multi-target. A crit heal doubles the healing and reduces target stress by 4. Restoration HoT can crit on each tick." Grepped `rules.json` for any `heal`/`crit`-named field that could back these three numbers — none exist (`player_crit`/`monster_crit` blocks only cover attack-crit stress relief/damage, not heal crit) — same "hardcoded in the binary" treatment as `HIDDEN_ACC_BONUS`/`TIER_TO_HERO_LEVEL`. Fixed: three new constants (`HEAL_CRIT_CHANCE_SINGLE_TARGET=0.12`, `HEAL_CRIT_CHANCE_PER_TARGET=0.05`, `HEAL_CRIT_STRESS_REDUCTION=4.0`) and a crit roll inside `_apply_heal` itself — new `crit_eligible: bool = False` and `is_multi_target: bool = False` params, opt-in per call site rather than auto-inferred from `performer.is_hero`, because `performer` also drives the pre-existing `produced_mult` calculation and one caller (`_tick_heal_dots`) must NOT pass a `performer` at all (`DotInstance` carries no caster reference — heal_dots is confirmed Flagellant-only real data, Reclaim, always self-targeting) while still needing the crit roll gated on the *receiving* hero. Wired into all four hero-cast heal paths: `_resolve_heal` (skill heals, `is_multi_target=len(targets)>1` computed once in `_execute_action`'s `is_heal` branch), `_resolve_self_action` (self-heals, always single-target), `_resolve_effect`'s `heal_min`/`heal_percent` branch (named-effect heals like Redeem/"Flag Last Gift" — treated as single-target per call since `_resolve_effect` resolves one `eff_target` at a time even for a group-dispatched effect, an acceptable simplification given the narrow confirmed Flagellant/DLC-only real scope), and `_tick_heal_dots` (`crit_eligible=combatant.is_hero`, decoupled from `performer` for the reason above). `_apply_heal` also re-checks `performer is None or performer.is_hero` internally as a belt-and-suspenders safety net, so a monster heal can never crit-heal even if some future caller passes `crit_eligible=True` by mistake — verified directly: a monster performer with `crit_eligible=True` and a rigged-to-always-crit RNG still applies the un-doubled amount. Tests: `test_heal_crit_doubles_amount_and_reduces_stress`, `test_heal_crit_single_vs_multi_target_rate`, `test_heal_crit_not_eligible_by_default_or_for_monster_heals` (`tests/engine/test_gaps.py`).
- **Underpowered-hero penalty was entirely unmodeled.** Wiki-confirmed via a direct fetch of `Resolve_Level`: "20 stress for every level of difference" plus "+25% stress for every level of difference" to incoming stress damage, applied per level a hero's resolve falls short of the expedition's own difficulty (Apprentice/Veteran/Champion = quest difficulty 1/3/5, the same 1-6 scale `hero_levels` already uses — confirmed via a second `rules.json` grep for `resolve`/`hero_level`/`quest`/`difficulty`, nothing). Fixed: new opt-in `CombatEngine.reset(quest_difficulty_level: Optional[int] = None)` param — `None` (default) means zero shortfall for every hero, byte-identical to before this param existed, same convention as `hero_levels`/`hero_skill_ids`/`carry_over`. When set, each hero's shortfall (`max(0, quest_difficulty_level - level)`) is computed alongside the existing resist-bonus-per-level logic and baked into the freshly-built `CombatantState` via `stress=UNDERLEVELED_STRESS_PER_LEVEL(20.0)*shortfall` and `stress_dmg_received_mult=1.0+UNDERLEVELED_STRESS_DMG_MULT_PER_LEVEL(0.25)*shortfall`, integrating for free with the pre-existing `_apply_stress` pipeline (`effective_mult = self._stress_damage_mult * target.stress_dmg_received_mult`) rather than adding a parallel code path. **Deliberately not wired into either expedition env's auto-sampling**: `TIER_TO_HERO_LEVEL` already pairs party level exactly to zone tier everywhere those envs construct a party, by design (see "Resolve Level" above) — so the shortfall this mechanic charges for is currently unreachable through this project's own envs, same "implemented for correctness, confirmed data-inert against current usage" category as `excessCritMult`. Only fires for a caller that explicitly passes a mismatched level, e.g. a manual `CombatEngine.reset()` call. Tests: `test_underleveled_penalty_defaults_to_zero`, `test_underleveled_penalty_scales_with_level_shortfall`, `test_underleveled_penalty_never_applies_when_at_or_above_difficulty` (`tests/env/test_hero_level.py`) — the scaling test needed a fast hero (Jester, SPD 9) rather than Crusader, for the same reason `test_carry_over_reset.py`/`test_heart_attack.py` already document: a slow hero lets `reset()`'s pre-hero-turn auto-resolve run a real enemy action first, and `cultist_brawler_A`'s own opening hit applies a real `stress_dmg_received_mult` debuff ("Brawler Rend Debuff 1", +10%) that would have silently contaminated the exact multiplier asserted on.
- **AoE bypassing Guard, and Stun breaking Guard — two separate real bugs under one wiki line, found together.** Wiki text: "Redirects the guarded unit's incoming attacks. AoE bypasses guard. Stun breaks guard." (a) `_resolve_guard_redirect` was called unconditionally at both call sites that resolve AoE targets (`_execute_action`'s attack branch, `_execute_enemy_skill`'s hero-targeting branch) — no `is_aoe` check existed anywhere, so a guarded hero standing in an AoE's target ranks was incorrectly protected from it, contrary to the real rule. Fixed: `_resolve_guard_redirect` gained an `is_aoe: bool = False` param, short-circuiting to the real target with no redirect; both call sites now pass their skill's own `is_aoe` flag. (b) Stun application (`_resolve_effect`'s `eff.stun_chance` branch) never touched `guard_target_id`/`guard_source_id` at all — a stunned guardian kept redirecting attacks meant for their ward for the rest of the guard's duration. The *direction* mattered enough to verify independently rather than assume: does stunning the GUARDIAN break the guard, or does stunning the GUARDED unit break the redirect? A second, targeted wiki fetch on `Status_Effects` confirmed unambiguously: *"whenever a guarding unit is stunned, the guard effect will immediately disappear from the protected unit"* — the guardian's stun is what breaks it. Fixed: new `_break_guard_if_guarding(combatant)` helper, called right after a stun lands; it also absorbed the pre-existing `is_clear_guarding` effect branch (identical logic — clear `combatant`'s own guard-on-someone-else plus the reverse link — previously duplicated inline, now shared). Tests: `test_aoe_attack_bypasses_guard` (verifies `_resolve_guard_redirect(target, is_aoe=True)` returns the real target, not the guardian, while `is_aoe=False` still redirects), `test_stunning_the_guardian_breaks_guard` (forces a guaranteed stun on a guardian via `stun_resist=-99`, asserts both `guard_target_id`/`guard_source_id` clear) (`tests/engine/test_gaps.py`).
- **Two "likely gaps" turned out already correct — verified, not assumed.** (1) Consecutive-stun resist stacking to 100%: `_apply_stun_recovery` already adds +50% `stun_resist` on every stun with no dedup guard, confirmed empirically (`0.4 -> 0.9 -> 1.4` across two stuns for a base-0.4-resist hero) — the resist roll (`rr < stun_resist`, `rr` uniform `[0,1)`) naturally saturates at guaranteed-resist past 1.0, matching the wiki with zero code change needed. (2) Multi-rank enemies being hit at most once per AoE: confirmed empirically against a real size-2 enemy (`brigand_blood_A`) and a real AoE skill (`zealous_accusation`) — a size-2 enemy is one `CombatantState` with a single `.position`, and AoE target resolution (`_resolve_action_targets`) filters `c.position in positions`, which structurally cannot double-match regardless of how many ranks the enemy visually spans.
- **One "likely gap" turned out moot by construction, not a bug.** Aegis + 0-damage attacks: `resolve_attack` floors all damage to `max(1, round(applied))` *before* the Aegis-block check ever runs, so Aegis always observes `applied >= 1` — there is no code path where "does a 0-damage hit still consume a block" could differ from current behavior, because a literal 0-damage hit can't reach that check. Left alone; flagged as a side-finding (15 real skill-levels — Hellion's Barbaric Yawp, Plague Doctor's Blinding Gas, Bounty Hunter's Flashbang, and 12 others — carry `dmg_mod <= -0.95`, i.e. near-zero-damage status attacks that this floor forces to deal a token 1 HP) without a confirmed wiki/doc source stating the real game intends a literal 0 rather than the same token minimum, so nothing changed.
- **One "likely gap" was already a documented, deliberate exclusion — not a fresh finding.** Surprise: unimplemented, but `ASSUMPTIONS.md`'s Torchlight section already excludes `monsters_surprised_increase`/`heroes_surprised_increase` (both present in the raw `rules.json` darkness table) as "dungeon exploration/loot mechanics with no analog in this single-encounter combat simulator." Re-confirmed the note is still accurate rather than re-flagging it as new.

## Known gaps (see `ASSUMPTIONS.md` for full detail)

Treat `ASSUMPTIONS.md` as the live source of truth for what's implemented vs stubbed — check it before assuming a mechanic works:
- Guard, Riposte, Stealth, Torchlight, Monster AI (including friendly targeting), and Resolve Level (`hero_levels`) **are** implemented. Guard now auto-expires after its real `.duration` (`_tick_guard_durations`); monster skill cooldowns are enforced (`MonsterBrain.skill_cooldowns`, `_tick_enemy_skill_cooldowns`); the stealth-attack crit bonus resolves to 0 because its buff id isn't present in the loaded buff data.
- Engine still defaults to skill level 4 / weapon/armour tier 4 (max) when `hero_levels` isn't passed — that's an intentional backward-compatible default, not a limitation (see Resolve Level above for how to opt into lower tiers).
- All 18 hero classes load, including DLC (shieldbreaker, musketeer, flagellant) — `HeroLoader.load_all` searches `heroes/` then falls back to a recursive `dlc/**/heroes/<id>/` scan. Modded heroes (Falconer/Black Mage/Red Mage/Pit Fighter) still don't load — no `.info.darkest` exists for them anywhere in the install, DLC or otherwise.
- Provisions (bandage/antivenom/holy_water/laudanum/food), reinforcements, the Move action, per-class on-crit passives, and the 3-tier Death's Door/heart-attack system **are** implemented — see "Full-expedition simulation" above. No camping-phase mechanics (firewood/camp skills), dungeon exploration/traversal, curios, torch decay over travel, resolve XP, or Crimson Curse. Torch/shovel/skeleton_key/medicinal_herbs/dog_treats (real provisions tied to exploration/curios/camping) also remain out of scope.
- `stress_dmg_received_percent` and all 4 real heal-modifier stat_types (`hp_heal_received_percent`, `hp_heal_percent`, `stress_heal_received_percent`, `stress_heal_percent`) are implemented (`CombatantState.stress_dmg_received_mult`/`hp_heal_received_mult`/`hp_heal_produced_mult`/`stress_heal_received_mult`/`stress_heal_produced_mult`). The engine used to check for a stat_type string, `heal_received_percent`, that appears in zero files in the entire game install — dead code that could never fire; the real keys were verified directly in `shared/buffs/base.buffs.json`.
- Consecutive-miss ACC buff (`rules.consecutive_miss_reduction_acc_buff`, +4%/miss) and excess-crit-to-damage overflow (`compute_excess_crit_bonus`, `excessCritMult`/`excessCritMax`) are both implemented in `engine/resolution.py`. The latter is confirmed data-inert — `excessCritMult` is `0.0` in every shipped `rules.json` (base + all mode overrides) — implemented for correctness rather than because it changes any number today.
- Corpses are cleared by effects with `.kill_enemy_types corpse` (`EffectDefinition.is_clear_corpses`), including size-2 corpses (both occupied positions).
- `combat_stat_multiply` buff reversal (`_reverse_buff_from_stats`) is now exact for duration-tracked buffs — `ActiveBuff.applied_delta` stores the precise integer delta `_apply_buff_to_stats` applied, subtracted back exactly rather than re-derived by dividing through the multiplier (which could drift by rounding).
- Not implemented, deliberately: The Hag's hero-capture ("captor") mechanic and the Ancestor's `death`-type phase-transition trigger — both need a new subsystem this simulator has no other support for, not just wiring existing data. See `ASSUMPTIONS.md`.
- Conditional trinket/quirk buffs (`rule_type`) **are** implemented for the 10 mechanically-common types (in_rank, meleeonly/rangedonly, hpbelow/hpabove, at_deaths_door, is_guarded, firstroundonly, stress_above, actorStatus/is_actor_status — see "Buffs are the universal modifier" above). Not implemented, deliberately: affliction/virtue **personality-type** buffs (Fearful/Paranoid/Masochistic/Abusive/Selfish/Depressed/Irrational; Stalwart/Courageous/Focused/Powerful/Vigorous) — `is_afflicted`/`is_virtuous` are still just flags with generic stress consequences, no "which specific personality did this hero break into" subsystem exists; same tier as Crimson Curse/camping. Also out of scope: monster-type-matchup rule types (extra resist/damage vs a specific monster tag) and town/camping/dungeon-traversal rule types.
- `CombatEngine.reset()` still defaults every hero to their full ~7-skill movepool when `hero_skill_ids` isn't passed — intentional backward-compatible default (see "Real per-comp skill loadouts" above for the real 4-skill restriction, now wired through `ExpeditionEnv`/`eval_expedition.py` automatically for the 131/145 comps with a matching loadout). Trinket/quirk ids captured by `CompLoadoutLoader` are now auto-applied too (`ExpeditionEnv`/`ExpeditionSequenceEnv`/`eval_expedition.py`, via the pre-existing `CombatEngine.apply_trinkets_and_quirks` — see "Real per-comp skill loadouts" above).

## Design rules (from `AGENTS.md`)

- CSVs are metadata-only; never treat a CSV value as combat-authoritative — always trace back to the game install source in the table above.
- No premature optimization; correctness and readability first.
- Reward shaping now exists (see Gym env section above) — if you touch it, keep `SHAPING_GAMMA` in sync with PPO's `gamma` in `train.py`, and don't add ad-hoc per-component weights outside the potential function `Φ` without re-checking the invariance argument in `ASSUMPTIONS.md`.
